# F00.04 Sign-in, account setup and sessions

**Outcome:** an invited person sets up one sign-in method with MFA from their emailed link, a passkey or a password
plus an authenticator app (D23, A22), and signs in and out on a phone or a laptop. Every request checks the session,
the 30-minute idle and 12-hour limits hold, a warning comes before the idle sign-out, and a person signed out mid-task
signs in again on the same screen.

## Stories
- S00.04.01 Session checked on every request
- S00.04.02 Set up my account with a passkey
- S00.04.03 Set up my account with a password and authenticator app
- S00.04.04 Sign in with a passkey
- S00.04.05 Sign in with email, password and code
- S00.04.06 Sign out
- S00.04.07 Warning before the idle sign-out
- S00.04.08 Sign in again where I was

## Feature checks
Owns these rows in [coverage.md](../coverage.md):
- design.md §14 M0 *Scope*, "Accounts: invite and setup (passkey, or password + TOTP), sign-in and sign-out, cookie
  sessions validated against the security stamp on every request, 30-minute idle timeout with warning, 12-hour
  limit." (Sending the invite is F00.06's; this feature covers accepting it.)
- design.md §14 M0 *Done when*: "A manager and a worker can each accept an invite and sign in with MFA on a phone and a
  laptop, in test, and sign in again a second time (both setup paths)"; "The 12-hour absolute limit and the `org_id`
  claim survive the per-request principal rebuild (tested)"; "Successful and failed sign-ins appear in the audit log";
  "HTTPS only with HSTS; the cookie is HttpOnly, Secure and SameSite=Strict; API responses carry `Cache-Control:
  no-store`."
- `sign-in.md › Sign in`, `sign-in.md › Sign in again, in place`, `sign-in.md › Setup`.
- `app-shell.md › Account and Sign out`, `app-shell.md › Session`.

Feature-level check: the first *Done when* line is a manual check in test, on a real phone and a laptop, for one
manager and one worker on each setup path. The worker's invite comes from the Users screen (S00.06.02). In the same
check, before M0 closes, the operator also runs by hand sign-in.md's screen-reader pass ("Tested with NVDA + Chrome
and VoiceOver on iOS before M0 closes", its checklist) over views A to G together: NVDA with Chrome on the laptop and
VoiceOver on the phone. The stories leave that pass to this check (coverage.md, `sign-in.md › Layout and
accessibility`).

From S00.04.06 (13 October 2026, its whole-branch review): the first real sign-outs in test are this check's, as
S00.04.06's after-deploy check meets only the `401` with no session. So in the same check each person signs out with
Sign out before their second sign-in, rather than closing the browser, and the check finds, for each, the logout's
`204` with `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "cookies", "storage"`, `401` from `/api/auth/me` after it, and one
`auth.signed_out` in the audit log, with the person as actor and entity.

From S00.04.07 (13 October 2026, its task 6): the first real Stay signed in in test is this check's too, as S00.04.07's
after-deploy check answers `/api/auth/me` and the ping in the browser. So in the same check, on the laptop, the
operator leaves a signed-in page untouched for 28 minutes, in front and with the screen kept awake (bringing the
window back to the front, or the page back into view, refreshes `/api/auth/me` and starts the 28 minutes again), and
finds the warning before the idle sign-out open over it, with focus on Stay signed in. NVDA with Chrome says its
sentence, "You'll be signed out in 2 minutes", "dialog" and Stay signed in as it opens, and, left open, each step's
sentence at 29:00, 29:20 and 29:40. Before 30:00, Stay signed in sends `POST /api/auth/ping`, which the developer
tools' Network panel shows answered `204`, and the warning closes, with focus back where it was. This is the
Definition of Done's screen reader spot check for this dialog ([README §9](../README.md#9-definition-of-done));
S00.04.07 checked the rest in CI and by hand in Chromium's accessibility tree (its Notes).

13 October 2026: these checks by hand, and the stories' own steps by hand in test, are gathered in one ordered
checklist, [ops/first-use-in-test.md](../../../ops/first-use-in-test.md), for the owner's first sign-in there. They no
longer hold a story open (owner, 12 October 2026; README §9); this feature's check is done when that checklist is.

## S00.04.01 Session checked on every request

```yaml
id: S00.04.01
type: enabler
title: Session checked on every request
status: done
points: 3
sprint: 2
depends_on: [S00.03.02, S00.03.04]
sources: [A24, A33, A34, D42, "design.md §5.9", "design.md §6.1", "design.md §6.2", "design.md §7.2", "design.md §7.3", "design.md §8.3", "design.md §8.4", "design.md §8.5", "design.md §8.6", "design.md §9.3", "design.md §9.7", "design.md §9.8", "design.md §14 M0", "mcp-server.md §3.5"]
github:
azure:
```

Enabler: Identity with the design.md §8.4 configuration: the sign-in manager (`CanSignInAsync` requires Status
Active; `auth_time` added once), the claims factory (`org_id`, role and display name rebuilt on every request), the
security stamp checked on every request with `auth_time` carried forward, the 12-hour limit, the `__Host-grow2notes`
cookie, HSTS, and `GET /api/auth/me`. So that deactivation, sign-in resets and role changes take effect on the next
request, and the session limits hold (A24, A34).

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- [design.md §14 M0](../../design.md#m0-skeleton-hosting-sign-in) *Done when*: the 12-hour limit and `org_id` line,
  and the HSTS and cookie parts of the HTTPS, HSTS, cookie and `no-store` line (HTTPS only is S00.02.02's, whose
  HTTPS criterion is ticked, and `no-store` is S00.01.01's, done). For the line "After deactivation, the user's next
  request is rejected", the stamp check it relies on; deactivating is F00.06's (coverage.md).
- design.md §8.3; §8.4's configuration of the sign-in manager, the claims factory, Identity's cookies, the security
  stamp check and the session cookie, and its *Claims* (the password, lockout, token provider and passkey settings
  come with the stories that first use them; see Notes); §8.5 (session cookie only, idle and absolute limits).
- design.md §7.2 *No login redirects*; §9.3 *Browser ↔ app*, its HSTS (the rest of that row is S00.02.02's); §9.8
  item 1, the session cookie's `SameSite=Strict`.

Specific to this story:
- [x] Given a session signed in 12 hours and 1 minute ago with steady activity, when it makes a request, then `401`
      (`FakeTimeProvider`).
- [x] Given a session idle for more than 30 minutes (tested at 30 minutes and 1 second), then `401`; a request at 29
      minutes renews it.
- [x] Given a manager since made a worker, when their open session calls a manager endpoint, then `403` on that very
      request.
- [x] Given a session whose user's security stamp has changed since its last request, as deactivation and a sign-in
      reset change it (design.md §8.6), when it makes a request, then `401`.
- [ ] `Set-Cookie` shows `__Host-grow2notes` with `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`, and no `Expires`
      or `Max-Age`; responses carry `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`.
- [x] `/api/auth/me` returns `userId`, `displayName`, `role`, `organisationName` and `today` (from `MelbourneClock`).
      `toReviewCount` is added in E03, and only when the role equals Manager (mcp-server.md §3.5 convention 4).

### Notes
- From S00.03.04 (PR #26 review, 9 October 2026): `Grow2NotesFactory` makes the test-only `TestSignIn` the default
  scheme, which signs a request in as the user that its `X-Test-User` header names. Keep it for the endpoint matrix
  while letting cookie-authenticated test requests through, for example with a policy scheme that forwards to
  `TestSignIn` only when `X-Test-User` is present and to Identity's cookie otherwise; then build `TestSignIn`'s
  principal with the real claims factory, so the matrix's claims are the real ones. Map `/api/auth/me` in the `/api`
  group (`MapApiGroup`) and add its matrix row. It is the first endpoint behind the fallback policy: until a scheme
  exists, a challenge has nothing to challenge with, so every endpoint is `AllowAnonymous` and every request matches
  one, and a protected endpoint answers `401` only once this story adds the cookie with its §8.4 events.
- Sprint 02, 9 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 after S00.03.04, as its plan allows
  (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the reference stories (releases.md), S00.03.02 (3 points) and S00.03.04
  (5 points), it stays at 3, at the top of that size. Its nine tasks configure Identity much as design.md §8.4 already
  writes it out, add one small endpoint and HSTS through ASP.NET Core's own middleware, add a cookie sign-in to the
  test fixtures and change the test-only one, and most of the work is the session tests on a fake clock. That is more
  than S00.03.02, which added no endpoint, but clearly less than S00.03.04, which changed how every error response is
  made, or Sign in with email, password and code, the 5-point reference, with its screen, two endpoints and four audit
  events. `depends_on` now names S00.03.04 (the Note above), whose policies, `/api` group, endpoint matrix and
  test-only sign-in its tasks build on. The linked §14 M0, §8.4 and §9.3 groups were narrowed to what this story
  builds; §7.2's *No login redirects* and §9.8 item 1 were linked; and a criterion for a changed security stamp was
  added, for coverage.md's M0 *Done when* row 2.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **Claims.** `Grow2NotesClaimsFactory` builds on Identity's `UserClaimsPrincipalFactory`, which gives the user ID,
    the user name, the email and the security stamp, and adds `org_id` under `TenantContext.OrganisationIdClaimType`,
    the role by its `UserRole` name in Identity's role claim type, which the policies compare exactly (S00.03.04), and
    the display name in a claim type of its own, `display_name`. Identity's name claim keeps the user name.
  - **`auth_time`** is the sign-in time from `TimeProvider`, in whole seconds since 1970, as OpenID Connect writes its
    `auth_time` claim. `SessionRules.ValidateAsync` rejects a principal that has none, so a cookie that did not come
    through `Grow2NotesSignInManager`, for example from `HttpContext.SignInAsync` called directly, ends on its next
    request rather than escaping the 12-hour limit (task 6 tests it); so every sign-in goes through the sign-in manager.
    Nothing calls `SignInManager.RefreshSignInAsync`, which also passes through `SignInWithClaimsAsync` and so would
    start a new 12 hours; a story that needs it carries `auth_time` forward first.
  - **The limits, to the second.** The cookie handler treats a session as expired only once its expiry has passed,
    and the 12-hour check rejects more than 12 hours (design.md §8.4, "exceeds"). So a request after exactly 30
    minutes idle, or exactly 12 hours after sign-in, is still let in, and the tests ask for the `401`s at 30 minutes
    and 1 second, and at 12 hours and 1 minute. These times are kept in whole seconds, so the session tests start the
    fake clock on a whole second, as 2026-10-09T13:30Z and `FakeTimeProvider`'s default do.
  - **The stamp check needs the clock to move.** Identity's validator checks the stamp only when more time than
    `ValidationInterval` has passed since the cookie was issued or last renewed, a time the cookie keeps to the second.
    With an interval of zero that is any time at all, so with the real clock every request is checked; but on a
    `FakeTimeProvider` that has not moved since the renewal, a request is not. The tests move the fake clock forward
    at least a second before each request, or a role change could go unseen.
  - **Signing in, in tests, with the real cookie.** No sign-in endpoint exists until S00.04.02, and the seeded users
    have no passkey or password. So a test maps a test-only endpoint, `AllowAnonymous` and outside the `/api` group,
    that loads a user and calls the app's `SignInManager.SignInAsync(user, isPersistent: false)`: the call that ends
    setup (design.md §8.1 step 5), which passes through `SignInWithClaimsAsync`, where every password, code and
    passkey sign-in ends. So the test gets the cookie a real sign-in issues, `auth_time` included, and the session
    tests run the app's own checks on it.
    Calling `HttpContext.SignInAsync` with Identity's scheme was not chosen: it skips the sign-in manager, so the
    session would have no `auth_time` and end on its next request (above), which is all task 6 uses it to show.
    `SignInAsync` does not ask `CanSignInAsync`, so the tests sign in Active users only, as setup does. The cookie is
    `Secure`, so these tests call `https://localhost`, as the antiforgery tests do.
  - **The test-only sign-in beside the cookie**, as the Note above suggests: `Grow2NotesFactory`'s default scheme
    becomes a policy scheme that forwards to `TestSignIn` when a request has `X-Test-User` or `X-Test-Role` (a role
    header alone still fails, as now) and to Identity's cookie otherwise, so a signed-out call meets the cookie's
    `401`, as in the deployed app. `TestSignIn` builds its principal with the app's claims factory and replaces only
    the role claim for `X-Test-Role`. It still skips the session checks, which the cookie tests cover.
    `AppWithoutDatabase` keeps hosting the app as it is deployed, with the cookie alone.
  - **The clock in tests.** A session test hosts the app with a `FakeTimeProvider` in place of `TimeProvider.System`.
    Identity's stamp validator and the cookie handler take `TimeProvider` from the services when their options name
    none, as `SessionRules` and `MelbourneClock` do, so one fake clock drives them all. Data Protection keeps reading
    the real clock, which does not matter here: its keys do not expire within a test run.
  - **HSTS** comes from ASP.NET Core's HSTS middleware (`AddHsts`, `UseHsts`), with a `MaxAge` of 365 days and
    `IncludeSubDomains`, and no `preload`, which design.md §9.7 does not give. §9.7 lists it with the headers that
    S06.01.04's middleware sets on every response, but this story and M0's *Done when* row 6 need it in M0
    (coverage.md), and the built-in middleware is the simplest way. It sends the header only on an HTTPS request, the
    only kind a browser heeds it on (RFC 6797 §7.2, §8.1), so it goes straight after `UseForwardedHeaders`, which
    makes a request that App Service's front end forwarded an HTTPS one. By default it leaves out `localhost`,
    `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]`, so development over `https://localhost` is never pinned, and its test calls another host
    name. A response the exception handler writes goes without it, because the handler clears the response's
    headers first: `DatabaseFailureHandler`'s `500`, an unhandled exception's `500`, and `BadHttpRequestHandler`'s
    `400` and `413`.
    That costs little, as a browser keeps the policy from any earlier response for a year; S06.01.04's header test,
    whose criteria list none of these, decides whether they get it too, and its middleware sets no second header
    (task 8's Note to it).
  - **`role` in `/api/auth/me`** is the `UserRole` name, `Worker` or `Manager`, as the role claim holds it and as most
    UX sketches compare it (`me.role === 'Manager'`). app-shell-nav.md's `Header` sketch compares `'manager'`, which
    a client `Me` type with `role` as a plain string would not catch, so task 5 hands S00.05.01 a Note to compare the
    name as the API gives it. The organisation's name is read through `ITenantContext`, so the endpoint also shows
    that the rebuilt principal's `org_id` is the request's tenant.
  - **Of design.md §8.4's block**, this story adds the sign-in manager, the claims factory, authentication with
    Identity's cookies, the stamp validator options and the session cookie. The password rules, lockout, the token
    providers and the passkey options come with the first story that uses each, such as S00.03.05's setup link and
    S00.04.05's lockout (its Notes).
  - `Grow2NotesSignInManager`, `Grow2NotesClaimsFactory`, `SessionRules` and `/api/auth/me` go in `Features/Auth/`
    (design.md §7.3: "sign-in, setup, passkeys, TOTP, session"), HSTS in `Program.cs`, and the policy scheme and the
    cookie sign-in in `tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/`.
- No audit event: design.md §5.4 has none for a session check. A session that ends at a limit or after a stamp change
  writes nothing; the change that rotated the stamp or changed the role writes its own (F00.06, S00.03.06), and the
  sign-in events are S00.04.02 to S00.04.06's.
- The stamp check runs on every request that carries the cookie, as design.md §8.4 asks, static files included:
  `UseAuthentication` authenticates each request, and the browser sends the cookie with the page's own asset
  requests. So each of a page load's few asset requests reads the user once and renews the cookie. That is accepted,
  as the hashed assets are fetched once and then cached. The cookie handler marks a response that renews the cookie
  `no-cache,no-store`, with `Pragma: no-cache` and an `Expires` in 1970. `CacheHeaders` has the last word on
  `Cache-Control`, and on a hashed asset also removes `Pragma` and `Expires`, without which Chromium and WebKit would
  revalidate the bundle on every signed-in load. A page or asset response that sets a cookie, as a renewal does, is
  `private` (`private, max-age=31536000, immutable` for an asset, `private, no-cache` for `index.html`), because a
  `Set-Cookie` does not stop a shared cache from keeping a response (RFC 9111 §7.3); one that sets none keeps design.md
  §7.2's `public` and `no-cache` (PR #28 review, A50).
- `deploy.yml`'s smoke test is unchanged: `/healthz` and `/api/auth/antiforgery` (S00.03.04) still show that the new
  build answers, and `/api/auth/me` needs a session. Nobody can sign in in test until S00.04.02, so the check after
  this story's deploy covers HSTS and a signed-out `/api/auth/me`, and the session criteria are proven in CI. The last
  task hands the in-test check of the session cookie's attributes to S00.04.02, as S00.03.03 handed on its
  `IpAddress` check, and the `Set-Cookie` criterion stays unticked until S00.04.02 has made it.
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.02 to S00.04.05 sign in through `Grow2NotesSignInManager`, setup with
  `SignInAsync` and the sign-in endpoints with Identity's sign-in methods, so `CanSignInAsync` and `auth_time` apply to
  every sign-in, and they answer `200 Me` with this story's `Me` type; S00.04.06 signs out through the sign-in manager;
  S00.04.07's `POST /api/auth/ping` needs no renewal of its own, because the stamp check renews the cookie on every
  request; S00.05.01 starts the shell from `/api/auth/me` and compares `role` with `'Manager'` (task 5's Note to it);
  F00.06's role changes and deactivation, and S00.03.06's sign-out of everyone, take effect on the next request through
  this check, and F00.06's deactivation test (coverage.md, M0 *Done when* row 2) can sign in with the cookie sign-in;
  E03 adds `toReviewCount` only when the role equals Manager; S06.01.04 keeps HSTS as it is here and adds the other
  design.md §9.7 headers (task 8's Note to it); and S02.02.01's "the integration test host can set the clock" can reuse
  the session tests' fake clock.

- 9 October 2026, PR #28 review: five findings were confirmed and are fixed here:
  - A signed-in asset response kept the cookie renewal's `Pragma: no-cache` and 1970 `Expires`, so Chromium and
    WebKit revalidated the bundle on every load; `CacheHeaders` now removes them and makes a response that sets a
    cookie `private` (the Note above, design.md A50).
  - Nothing showed that the app's `OnRedirectToAccessDenied` replaced the cookie's redirect, or that the cookie is
    `Secure` on a plain-HTTP request: the role-change test now checks the `403` has no `Location`, and a sign-in over
    HTTP checks the cookie's attributes.
  - The criteria that the tests prove were not ticked; all but the `Set-Cookie` one, which S00.04.02 checks in test,
    are ticked now.
  - S02.02.01 had no Note that the test host's settable clock exists (`TestClock`), and F00.01's Note still gave it to
    S02.02.01; each has a dated Note now. S00.04.04 also has one on the passkey sign-in on runtimes before 10.0.4.

- 9 October 2026, task 9: PR #28 merged as cf5200c; CI run 37872635138 (400 tests and both Playwright tests passed)
  and Deploy run 37872942861 succeeded, every step green. The app deploy needed no retry, the smoke test matched
  `index.html`'s `Last-Modified` on its first attempt, `/healthz` answered `Healthy`, and the antiforgery check got
  `204` with the token header and the `__Host-grow2notes-xsrf` cookie with its four attributes. Requests that change
  nothing, made after the deploy, each carried exactly one `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000;
  includeSubDomains`: `/`, `/api/auth/antiforgery`'s `204`, `/healthz`, the hashed bundle and `GET /api/auth/me`. The
  bundle is still `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, with no `Pragma`, `Expires` or `Set-Cookie`. `GET
  /api/auth/me` with no cookie, and with a garbled `__Host-grow2notes`, got `401` problem+json, not `500`, with
  `no-store`, no `Location` and no `Set-Cookie`. Plain HTTP gets App
  Service's `301` to HTTPS without the header: the front end answers it before the app. Application Insights had no
  exception and no `5xx` from the deploy to the checks. The other criteria are proven in CI (`SessionLimitTests`,
  `NextRequestTests` and `MeEndpointTests`), as are the cookie's attributes and HSTS (`CookieSignInTests`,
  `HstsTests`). In test, the `Set-Cookie` criterion's HSTS half is now shown, but its cookie half needs a sign-in,
  which nobody can make there before S00.04.02; so it stays unticked, handed to S00.04.02 (its Notes), which ticks it
  here with a Note giving the run ID.
- 13 October 2026: the owner decided on 12 October 2026 that checks by hand in test are nice-to-haves that must not hold
  a story open (README §9), so S00.04.02 is closed without the first sign-in in test that was to read this story's
  `Set-Cookie`. That check is now step 4 of [ops/first-use-in-test.md](../../../ops/first-use-in-test.md), the one
  checklist for the owner's first sign-in there. The `Set-Cookie` criterion stays unticked until that step is done; its
  cookie half is proven in CI (`CookieSignInTests`) and its HSTS half in test (the 9 October Note above).

### Tasks
- [x] Add `Grow2NotesClaimsFactory` and `Grow2NotesSignInManager` in `Features/Auth/`, registered as design.md §8.4
      registers them (`AddSignInManager`, `AddClaimsPrincipalFactory`): the factory adds `org_id`, the role and the
      display name to Identity's own claims (see Notes); the sign-in manager's `CanSignInAsync` admits only a user
      whose Status is Active, and its `SignInWithClaimsAsync` adds `auth_time`, the sign-in time from `TimeProvider`,
      which task 4 tests through the first cookie sign-in; tests on the SQL Server container: the factory's principal
      for each seeded worker and manager holds their user ID, `org_id`, role name and display name, and
      `CanSignInAsync` is true for an Active user and false for an Invited and a Deactivated one, users the test adds
      to organisation A
- [x] Make Identity's cookie the app's authentication scheme with design.md §8.4's settings
      (`AddAuthentication(IdentityConstants.ApplicationScheme).AddIdentityCookies()`, `SecurityStampValidatorOptions`
      with a zero `ValidationInterval`, `ConfigureApplicationCookie` with `__Host-grow2notes`, `Secure` always,
      `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, 30 minutes sliding), with `SessionRules` in `Features/Auth/`: `ValidateAsync`
      awaits the stamp check, then rejects and signs out a principal whose `auth_time` is more than 12 hours before now
      on `TimeProvider`, or that has none, and `CarryForward` copies `auth_time` to the rebuilt principal, all of which
      task 6 tests on the cookie; the cookie's `OnRedirectToLogin` and `OnRedirectToAccessDenied` answer `401` and
      `403`, which the status code pages make problem details (design.md §7.2); bring up to date what says the app has
      no scheme (`Program.cs`'s comment, `SignedOutTests`' summary, `tests/README.md`), and test in `AppWithoutDatabase`
      that a signed-out request to a test-only endpoint behind the fallback policy gets `401` problem+json with
      `no-store`, not `500`
- [x] Keep the test-only sign-in beside the cookie (see Notes): `Grow2NotesFactory`'s default scheme becomes a policy
      scheme that forwards to `TestSignIn` when a request has `X-Test-User` or `X-Test-Role` and to Identity's cookie
      otherwise, and `TestSignIn` builds its principal with the app's claims factory, replacing only the role claim
      for `X-Test-Role`; check that the endpoint matrix, `PoliciesOnSqlServerTests` and every other test still pass,
      the matrix's signed-out calls now refused by the cookie; and bring `tests/README.md`'s *Calling as a seeded user*
      up to date
- [x] Add the test-only cookie sign-in in `tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/` (see Notes): an endpoint that a test
      maps, which signs a given user in with the app's `SignInManager.SignInAsync(user, isPersistent: false)`, and a
      helper that calls it from an HTTPS client, which keeps the cookie; and a way to host the app on a
      `FakeTimeProvider`; tests: the sign-in's `Set-Cookie` holds `__Host-grow2notes` with `Secure`, `HttpOnly`,
      `SameSite=Strict` and `Path=/`, and no `Domain`, `Expires` or `Max-Age`; a later request with that cookie alone,
      after the clock moves a second, gets `200` from a test-only endpoint behind the fallback policy, whose user
      holds the factory's claims and an `auth_time` equal to the sign-in time on the fake clock; and the cookie
      renewed on that response has the same attributes; say in `tests/README.md` how a test signs in with the cookie
      and sets the clock
- [x] Add `GET /api/auth/me` in `Features/Auth/`, mapped in the `/api` group behind the fallback policy (design.md
      §6.2): `{userId, displayName, role, organisationName, today}` for the signed-in user, with `role` the role
      claim's `UserRole` name, the organisation's name read through `ITenantContext`, `today` from `MelbourneClock`
      as `YYYY-MM-DD`, and no `toReviewCount` (E03 adds it), in a `Me` type that setup and the sign-in endpoints also
      answer with (S00.04.02 to S00.04.05); add its matrix row (`401` signed out, `200` for the other three callers)
      and a signed-out `401` problem to `SignedOutTests`; tests with the cookie sign-in: A's worker and A's manager
      each get their own ID, name and role, and A's name, B's manager gets B's, `today` is Melbourne's date where it
      differs from UTC's (2026-10-10 at 2026-10-09T13:30Z on the fake clock), a manager's answer has no
      `toReviewCount` property, and the response carries `Cache-Control: no-store` (design.md §6.1); and add a Note to
      S00.05.01 (see Notes): compare `me.role` with `'Manager'`, as the API gives it, not app-shell-nav.md's
      `'manager'`, for example by typing the client's `role` as user-management.md's `Role` so the sketch's spelling
      does not compile
- [x] Write the session-limit tests with the cookie sign-in on a fake clock, moved forward before every request (see
      Notes): a request after 29 minutes idle gets `200` and a renewed cookie, so a request 29 minutes after that gets
      `200` too; after 30 minutes and 1 second idle, `401`; with a request every 20 minutes from sign-in, the request
      at exactly 12 hours gets `200` and the one at 12 hours and 1 minute gets `401`, which shows that `auth_time` is
      carried through every rebuild; every `/api/auth/me` on the way names the user's organisation, which shows
      that `org_id` survives the rebuild too (design.md §14 M0 *Done when*); and a cookie with no `auth_time`, from a
      test-only endpoint that calls `HttpContext.SignInAsync(IdentityConstants.ApplicationScheme, …)` with the
      factory's principal, gets `401` on its next request after the clock moves, with a `Set-Cookie` that clears
      `__Host-grow2notes`, which shows that a sign-in outside `Grow2NotesSignInManager` cannot escape the 12-hour
      limit
- [x] Write the next-request tests with the cookie sign-in on a fake clock, on users the test adds to organisation A
      (tests/README.md's seeded-rows rule): after `UpdateSecurityStampAsync`, as deactivation and a sign-in reset
      rotate the stamp (design.md §8.6), the next request gets `401`; a manager whose session got `200` from a
      test-only endpoint with the `Manager` policy, made a worker through `UserManager` with the stamp unchanged, gets
      `403` from it on the next request; and a changed display name shows in the next `/api/auth/me`
- [x] Add HSTS (see Notes): `AddHsts` with a `MaxAge` of 365 days and `IncludeSubDomains`, and `UseHsts` straight
      after `UseForwardedHeaders`; test over HTTPS at a host name other than `localhost`: `index.html`, a hashed
      asset, `/api/auth/antiforgery`'s `204`, `/api/auth/me`'s `401`, an unknown `/api` path's `404` and `/healthz`
      each carry `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`; a plain-HTTP request gets none; and
      a request from a front-end address with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, as `ForwardedHeadersTests` sends one, gets
      it; and add a Note to S06.01.04 (see Notes): HSTS comes from this story's `UseHsts`, sent only over HTTPS and
      never to `localhost`, so its middleware sets no second `Strict-Transport-Security` and its header test looks for
      it over HTTPS at another host name; and a response the exception handler writes (a `500`, or
      `BadHttpRequestHandler`'s `400` or `413`) goes without it, which S06.01.04 either accepts or fixes by adding
      those to its header test and setting HSTS in its own middleware in place of `UseHsts`
- [x] After the deploy to test, check with requests that change nothing: `/`, `/api/auth/antiforgery` and `/healthz`
      carry `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`; `GET /api/auth/me` with no cookie
      answers `401` problem+json with `no-store`; and the deploy's smoke test passed; record the run IDs in Notes, and
      that the criteria are proven in CI; and add a Note to S00.04.02 to read the `Set-Cookie` of its first sign-in in
      test, then tick this story's `Set-Cookie` criterion with a Note giving the run ID, as S00.03.03's `IpAddress`
      check was handed on

## S00.04.02 Set up my account with a passkey

```yaml
id: S00.04.02
type: story
title: Set up my account with a passkey
status: done
points: 5
sprint: 2
depends_on: [S00.03.05, S00.04.01, S00.01.04, S00.01.05]
sources: [D21, D23, D42, D67, D70, D71, A22, A23, A35, A38, "design.md §4.1", "design.md §5.4", "design.md §5.9", "design.md §6.2", "design.md §6.9", "design.md §7.5", "design.md §8.1", "design.md §8.4", "design.md §9.8", "ux/screens/sign-in.md", "ux/components/passkey-flows.md", "ux/screens/app-shell.md", "ux/components/app-shell-nav.md", "ux/components/microcopy.md"]
github:
azure:
```

As an invited worker or manager, I want to set up my account with a passkey from my emailed link,
so that I sign in with my face, fingerprint or phone PIN and never need a password.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §4.1 *Setting up an account* steps 1 to 3 and 5, and the expired-link, timed-out and in-app-browser rows.
- design.md §8.1 steps 2 to 5, for the passkey.
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Acceptance criteria › Setup, these bullets: "Opening
  `/setup#u=…&t=…`…", "`410` shows…", "A `401` from any setup call…", "Opening `/setup`, `/setup/password` or
  `/setup/app` with nothing in memory…", "Network failure on the link check…", "View E shows…", "With the CDP virtual
  authenticator…", "With WebAuthn unavailable…", and, for the passkey path, "Both setup paths end on Today…" and
  "After setup, signing in a second time works…" (here if S00.04.04's endpoint is on main when task 7 is written,
  otherwise S00.04.04's; see Notes).
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Privacy and storage, its first three bullets, and › Layout and
  accessibility, its first two, each for views D and E (the second's NVDA and VoiceOver pass is this feature's check
  before M0 closes, under *Feature checks* above, as the Notes say).

Specific to this story:
- [x] `POST /api/auth/setup/start` checks the token once and issues the 30-minute enrolment cookie, valid only on
      `/api/auth/setup/*`; later steps check the cookie and Status Invited, not the token.
- [x] The passkey creation options require user verification and a discoverable credential, for the environment's
      own domain.
- [x] Completion is one transaction: the passkey stored, Status Active, `ActivatedAtUtc` and `EmailConfirmed` set,
      the stamp rotated, the person signed in, and `auth.setup_completed` written.
- [x] Given a completed setup, when the same link is opened again, then `410 auth.setup_link_invalid`; the same for a
      link older than 7 days.
- [x] The setup endpoints set the tenant from the loaded user, never from the request (design.md §5.9 item 1).

### Notes
- Not Ready until the owner settles sign-in.md open question 7: the fixed passkey `name` sent with `POST
  /api/auth/setup/passkey` (suggested "Grow2Notes"; never shown).
- Until S00.05.01 builds the `/` route, "ends on Today" means setup ends by going to `/`, which S00.01.01's
  client-route fallback serves. S00.05.01 re-checks that both setup paths land on its Today heading, and E02 builds
  Today itself.
- Which phone email apps can create passkeys is the go-live device check in `sign-in.md › Layout and accessibility`
  (a Definition of Done row). S06.02.02 does it once, before go-live.
- Carried from S00.02.02 on 7 October 2026: the signed-in half of its criterion "Restarting the web app keeps people
  signed in (the key ring is in SQL)", because this is the first story that signs someone in, in test. After this
  story's deploy, with someone signed in through setup, restart `app-grow2notes-test` and check that the same session
  still gets `200` from `GET /api/auth/me` without signing in again; then tick S00.02.02's criterion with a Note. Its
  key-ring half is checked in S00.02.02 (Sprint 02 Q6).
- Carried from S00.03.03 on 9 October 2026: the in-test check of its criterion "`IpAddress` is the real client IP
  behind App Service's front end (forwarded headers)", because this story's `auth.setup_completed` is the first audit
  row a request writes in test. After this story's deploy, read that row's `IpAddress`, as
  `infra/sql/check-database.sql` reads the database or through the audit extraction available then, and confirm it is
  the tester's own address, not a front-end one; then tick S00.03.03's criterion with a Note giving the run ID. If it
  is not, the front end calls from outside the known networks, and this story fixes `KnownIPNetworks` in `Program.cs`.
- Carried from S00.04.01 on 9 October 2026: the in-test check of the cookie half of its criterion "`Set-Cookie` shows
  `__Host-grow2notes` with `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`, and no `Expires` or `Max-Age`", because
  this story's setup is the first sign-in in test; its HSTS half was checked after S00.04.01's deploy. After this
  story's deploy, read the `Set-Cookie` of the first sign-in in test, on the `200 Me` that ends setup: it must be
  `__Host-grow2notes` with `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` and `Path=/`, and no `Domain`, `Expires` or
  `Max-Age`; then tick S00.04.01's criterion with a Note giving the run ID.
- Carried from S00.01.04 on 10 October 2026, from S00.03.04's Note on its task 8: HTTPS in CI's end-to-end run, as
  this story's end-to-end test is the first to send a change through the `api()` wrapper, and S00.01.04 has none of
  its own. CI's end-to-end run serves `http://localhost:5000`, but the antiforgery endpoint and every token check need
  HTTPS, because the antiforgery cookie is `Secure`. Before the wrapper calls them there, with this story's
  `POST /api/auth/setup/start`, the run serves HTTPS, or sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, which the app trusts from
  loopback, and the browser must then still keep the `Secure` cookie. The wrapper fetches the token from
  `GET /api/auth/antiforgery`, from the SPA's own origin, before the first `POST`, `PUT` or `DELETE`, and a token
  request that fails fails its change.
- Carried from S00.01.04 on 10 October 2026: the setup steps' antiforgery token, for this story to settle. The
  wrapper fetches its token before `/api/auth/setup/start`, so it is bound to no user, and drops it only after a `2xx`
  from the four calls that sign someone in (`/api/auth/login/totp`, `/api/auth/passkey`, `/api/auth/setup/password`
  and `/api/auth/setup/passkey`). If the enrolment cookie becomes the request's user on `/api/auth/setup/*` (through a
  policy naming its scheme, say) while `GET /api/auth/antiforgery` does not see it (through its `Path` or scheme),
  every later setup `POST` gets a `400`, and the retry's fresh token, anonymous too, fails the same way. So either the
  setup endpoints check the token against the identity the antiforgery endpoint sees, or the antiforgery endpoint
  sees the enrolment cookie and the wrapper also drops the token after a `2xx` from `/api/auth/setup/start`. And a
  failed setup answers with a `code`, such as sign-in.md's *Passkey not set up* `400` from `/api/auth/setup/passkey`,
  since the wrapper reads a `400` with none as a stale token and sends the request once more, with the same
  credential.
- Carried from S00.01.04 on 9 October 2026 (its branch review): test's paused database outlasts the `api()` wrapper.
  The free-offer database pauses after an idle hour and takes about a minute to resume (deploy.yml's note on error
  40613), while the wrapper gives a change 10 s and a query about 21 s with its one silent retry, and the server,
  retrying through `EnableRetryOnFailure`, carries on. A signed-in request never meets it paused, as a session ends
  after 30 idle minutes, but this story's `POST /api/auth/setup/start` is anonymous and reads it, as do the first
  sign-in calls (`POST /api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/passkey`). So the first of them after a pause says "Not [done]:
  no connection. Try again." while the server may still finish it: a setup start reported as failed may have used
  up its link, so that pressing again gets `410`. The deploy's "Migrate the database" step wakes the database, so a
  check soon after the deploy finds it awake; a check through the wrapper run more than an idle hour later first
  wakes it with a `GET /healthz/ready`, which changes nothing, and waits for `200`. Treat "no connection" during a
  cold start as that, not as a defect. Production's S0 database does not pause.
- Carried from S00.03.05 on 9 October 2026: the first run of `admin bootstrap` in test, and the in-test proof of two
  of its criteria. This story's after-deploy check first runs `admin bootstrap` in test (operator, by hand, as
  `ops/break-glass.md` says, with made-up names and an `example.org` address, A35), opens the printed link and
  completes setup with it; then it ticks S00.03.05's first criterion with a Note giving the run ID. Its tests show
  that no setup step before completion changes the security stamp, and it then ticks S00.03.05's third criterion
  (task 9).
  `setup/start` reads `u` and `t` from the link's fragment with `URLSearchParams`, which reads the escaped token back
  exactly, and verifies the token with `SetupTokenProvider`'s name and purpose constants; the passkey options can take
  `IdentityPasskeyOptions.ServerDomain` from `App:Origin`'s host, so the links and the passkeys name one domain.
  S00.03.05's task 6, the operator's rehearsal in the SSH console, was dropped by the owner on 12 October 2026 (its
  Notes), so this first run is itself the check that the console has what the command needs. Follow
  `ops/break-glass.md`'s check by name before running the command: a missing connection string, client ID,
  `App__Origin` or identity endpoint would stop the command with nothing written, but a missing
  `DataProtection__KeyVaultKeyUri` would not, so a `MISSING` there means stop and run nothing. If that check, or the
  runbook's way into the console, fails, this story finds what works and puts it in `ops/break-glass.md`.
- Owner answer, 9 October 2026: the fixed passkey `name` sent with `POST /api/auth/setup/passkey` is "Grow2Notes",
  never shown (D70, now in design.md §6.2), which settles the open question 7 Note above. The setup copy marked (P) in
  sign-in.md is approved as written (D67).
- Owner answer, 11 October 2026: design.md §7.5's generated API types are dropped (D71): there is no openapi-typescript
  or openapi-fetch, and the API serves no OpenAPI document. This story's calls name each answer's TypeScript type by
  hand through S00.01.04's `api()` wrapper (`Me` for the `200` that ends setup), as every later screen's do. This
  settles releases.md's Sprint 05 question on §7.5, which named this story as the first screen.
- From S00.01.05 (9 October 2026, its branch review): this is the first story in the backlog order that words an API
  error code, `410 auth.setup_link_invalid` (sign-in.md's *Link expired, used or replaced*), so it builds
  `src/copy/apiErrors.ts` as microcopy.md §8 sketches it, with its tests; S00.01.05 left it to its first user, so that
  no part of the copy module sits unused. §8 types the map against the union of design.md §6.9's codes, so that a code
  with no words fails the build. Whether the union starts with all of §6.9's codes, each worded here, or with this
  story's own, each later story adding its codes, is for this story's refinement to settle, and its size with it.
- From S00.05.01 (12 October 2026, its task 7): S00.05.01's fourth criterion, that both setup paths end on its `/`
  route (sign-in.md › Setup, "Both setup paths end on Today…"), is checked by whichever of S00.05.01 and this story
  merges second, and this story was not on `main` when S00.05.01 checked. So this story checks it when it is built:
  its end-to-end setup test finds, after the passkey path, the address `/`, Today's `<h1>` for the `today` of the
  `200 Me` that ends setup (today.md component 3), and the title "Grow2Notes – Today". That `200 Me` goes into the
  shell's `['me']` with `queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], me)`, as sign-in.md interaction 12 does for the password
  path, because a shell that stays mounted from `/setup` would otherwise keep the `401` its `['me']` got there
  (`staleTime: Infinity`), and `/` would show the signed-out frame. Once that check passes in CI and S00.04.03's has
  too, it ticks S00.05.01's fourth criterion, with a Note there giving both run IDs. This replaces the sentence in the
  Note above that S00.05.01 re-checks the landing.
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 after S00.01.05, as its plan allows
  (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and S00.04.05
  (5 points), it stays at 5, at the top of that size. Its eleven tasks add three endpoints over Identity, with an
  enrolment cookie of their own and one audit event written in one transaction; a screen of two views and three
  whole-page messages with the passkey ceremony; the signed-out frame and the routes that setup needs; an end-to-end
  test that makes a real passkey; and one step by the operator in test. That is about as much as Sign in with email,
  password and code, the 5-point reference, whose screen has two steps over two endpoints, four audit events and the
  lockout and timing tests: here Identity checks the passkey and SimpleWebAuthn encodes it, and views F and G, with
  their three endpoints, are S00.04.03's. It is not clearly an 8 to split. `depends_on` now names S00.04.01, whose
  sign-in manager, session cookie and `Me` completion uses, and S00.01.04 and S00.01.05, whose `api()` wrapper,
  shared parts and copy module the screen uses, beside S00.03.05; all are done but S00.01.05, earlier in this sprint
  (merged in PR #37, its check after the deploy to come). `sources` adds D67, D70 and D71, which settle its wording
  and API details, A35, design.md §6.9, §7.5 and §9.8, app-shell.md and app-shell-nav.md for the frame and the page
  titles, and microcopy.md for `apiErrors.ts`. The linked criteria stand; the passkey path's second sign-in waits on
  S00.04.04's endpoint (below). Nothing here checks for the parent company's name (D72), and nothing is rehearsed in
  the SSH console (S00.03.05's Notes): the operator's one step in test is the first real `admin bootstrap`, without
  which no one can sign in there, after `ops/break-glass.md`'s check of the six settings by name, which stays because
  a missing `DataProtection__KeyVaultKeyUri` would let the command add an unwrapped key to the key ring (S00.03.05's
  owner decision of 12 October 2026). Nor does it run a screen reader: the Definition of Done's spot check is for a
  changed form or dialog, which views D and E are not, sign-in.md's NVDA and VoiceOver runs come before M0 closes, in
  this feature's check over views A to G (*Feature checks*, named there by the branch review), and tasks 6 and 7 test
  view E's accessible names and its alert.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **The enrolment cookie** is a cookie scheme of its own in `Features/Auth/`, `__Secure-grow2notes-setup`, with
    `Path=/api/auth/setup`, so that the browser sends it to the setup endpoints only (design.md §8, "valid only on
    `/api/auth/setup/*`"), and `Secure`, `HttpOnly` and `SameSite=Strict`, as the session cookie is; a `__Host-` name
    would need `Path=/`. It holds the user's ID, and since the branch review their security stamp (A53, its Note
    below), and is a session cookie whose ticket ends 30 minutes after the link check, not sliding, so that the window
    has one end, as the two-factor cookie's has (D69). The setup endpoints read it themselves, with
    `AuthenticateAsync` on its scheme, never through a policy, so it never becomes the request's user: the antiforgery
    token that the wrapper fetched before `setup/start`, for the user that the antiforgery endpoint saw, still checks
    on every setup `POST`, the first of the two ways in the S00.01.04 Note above, and the wrapper is unchanged. Each
    step after `start` answers `401`, with no code (design.md §6.9), when there is no valid cookie or its user is no
    longer Invited with its stamp, and sign-in.md shows "Your setup session timed out" for it.
  - **A link that does not check**, whether its ID is not a GUID or names no user, its token does not verify, or its
    user is not Invited, gets the one `410 auth.setup_link_invalid`, so the answer never says whether an account
    exists. `userId` and `token` are strings, so a garbled link gets the `410` too, not a binding `400`, which the
    wrapper would send again as a stale token.
  - **The passkey options** are design.md §8.4's block, with `ServerDomain` the host of `App:Origin`, as S00.03.05's
    Note above suggests, so that the links and the passkeys name one domain: the test address in test (D62), the
    production domain there (A38, S00.02.04), `localhost` in CI's end-to-end run and `grow2notes.example` in the
    integration tests. `AuthenticatorTimeout` keeps Identity's 5 minutes (passkey-flows.md *Implementation notes*), and
    the user entity is the user's ID, email and display name, with no organisation name (D42). S00.04.04 signs in
    under the same block.
  - **A refused passkey** gets `422 validation.failed` with `errors.credentialJson`, as D70 answers a wrong setup code:
    when the attestation fails, when no ceremony is under way (Identity throws), and when the options were made for
    another user than the enrolment cookie's. A `400` with no code would be read by the wrapper as a stale token and
    sent again (the S00.01.04 Note above), and design.md §6.9 has no `400` code for it, so the existing code serves.
    The screen shows sign-in.md's *Passkey not set up* for it; design.md §6.2's row, a new §13 default, sign-in.md's
    state and passkey-flows.md's *States* row say so (task 9).
  - **`name`** is neither read nor checked. The SPA sends D70's "Grow2Notes", as design.md §6.2's body has it, but the
    endpoint binds only `credentialJson` (the app's JSON skips a member it does not bind) and stores every passkey
    under "Grow2Notes", so no other words reach it and there is no check to test. `credentialJson` is `[Required]`, so
    a body without it gets the built-in validation's `422` with `errors.credentialJson`, as a refused one does.
  - **Completion's order.** One `InTransactionAsync` (design.md §5.9), inside `ITenantContext.Use` for the user's
    organisation, loads the user again, as the helper clears the change tracker on each attempt, then stores the
    passkey, sets Status Active, `ActivatedAtUtc` and `EmailConfirmed`, rotates the security stamp and writes
    `auth.setup_completed`, with the user as its actor, entity type `User` and the user's ID, and no `Details`. A
    failed Identity result in it throws, so nothing commits. The sign-in comes after the commit, with
    `SignInAsync(user, isPersistent: false)`: the cookie must hold the rotated stamp, or the next request's stamp check
    would end the session at once, and a retried attempt then signs in only once. The answer is `200 Me` from the
    user's row, as the sign-in endpoints of S00.04.04 and S00.04.05 answer; whichever comes first adds the code that
    builds it, and the others reuse it. The sign-in and `Me` stay inside the `Use` block, after the transaction, as
    the request has no session and `Me`'s organisation name is read for the tenant (`MeEndpoint`).
  - **The integration tests' passkey handler.** Identity registers `IPasskeyHandler<ApplicationUser>` with the sign-in
    manager (the probe below), so the completion tests replace it with a test-only one that accepts a made-up
    credential, giving a made-up public key and the user entity the test names, or refuses it; they test the
    endpoint's checks, its transaction and its audit event. The end-to-end test makes a real passkey with Chromium's
    virtual authenticator, which Identity's own handler verifies. A real attestation made in .NET would need a CBOR
    writer and an authenticator of the tests' own.
  - **`src/copy/apiErrors.ts`** settles the S00.01.05 Note above: its `ApiErrorCode` union starts with this story's
    one code, and each later story adds the codes it words, as the server's `ErrorCode` holds only the codes the app
    answers. The map is checked with `satisfies Record<ApiErrorCode, …>`, so that a code in the union with no words
    fails the type check, while each entry keeps the shape its screen needs: the `410`'s is a whole-page message, a
    heading and one sentence (sign-in.md *Link expired, used or replaced*). microcopy.md §8's sketch says so (task 9).
  - **The frame and the routes.** React Router's data router (8.4.0, as S00.05.01's refinement has it; design.md §7.5,
    app-shell-nav.md *Implementation notes*), with only what setup needs: one root layout with the skip link, the header
    with the wordmark as plain text, and `<main id="main-content" tabindex="-1">` (app-shell.md components 1 to 3); page
    titles as app-shell.md component 3 gives them, so that S00.05.01 writes the same: one `PageName` union holding its
    whole list, each route declaring its name once with `handle: { pageName }`, and the root layout setting
    `document.title` from the deepest match's name through app-shell-nav.md's `usePageTitle`, to "Grow2Notes – {name}",
    or to "Grow2Notes", as `index.html` has it, on a route with none; and `/setup`, `/setup/password` and `/setup/app`,
    each named "Set up your account" (sign-in.md component 1), under a setup layout that holds `{email, displayName}` in
    memory, which leaving the layout drops (sign-in.md *Interactions* 13). Every other path keeps the placeholder page
    until S00.05.01 builds `/` and S00.05.03 *Page not found*. Setup renders without waiting for `/me`, with the
    wordmark as text whoever is signed in (app-shell-nav.md *Where it's used*), which task 9 tells S00.05.01 and
    S00.05.03. If `origin/main` has S00.05.01's router and shell when task 4 starts, it adds the setup routes to them,
    keeping one version. S00.05.01 merged first, so the rebase did that (its Note below).
  - **"Error: " in the page title** (sign-in.md component 1's "After a failure", and its checklist) shows on setup
    while a failure sentence does: the link check's "Your setup link was not checked: …" and view E's request-failure
    alert (a passkey not set up, a failed request, a `429`). The whole-page messages keep the plain title, as each is
    a page of its own whose `h1` says what happened. The prefix goes on as `ErrorSummary` already puts it (S00.01.04),
    in front of whatever title the root layout set, and comes off when the failure clears or its screen goes; that
    effect moves into one small hook that both use, so the app has one way to add it, and `usePageTitle` is built
    without app-shell-nav.md's optional `hasError`, which would be a second (task 9 says so there).
  - **The fragment** goes with React Router's `navigate('/setup', { replace: true })`, which replaces the history entry
    as design.md §8.1's `history.replaceState` does while keeping the router's own state, which is all that
    `history.state` may hold (app-shell.md); sign-in.md's step 7 says so (task 9).
  - **`/setup/password` and `/setup/app`** show only the no-link message here, for the linked bullet, and S00.04.03
    builds views F and G on them. Until it merges, the password option leads to that message even with setup in
    memory, which test, holding made-up data only (A35), can bear.
  - **The passkey helpers** are a shared piece: passkey-flows.md's *Implementation notes* sketch them as shared helpers
    ("Setup uses the same pattern"), and sign-in.md takes passkey-flows.md as one of its components. View E's one
    options object (stamped when its request is sent, a pending prefetch reused by a press rather than a second request,
    4 minutes at most, spent once its credential is sent) and the cancel on unmount come from passkey-flows.md's helpers
    in `src/features/auth/passkey.ts`, where S00.04.04 places them, and are not built again in the view. If
    `origin/main` has them when task 6 starts, it reuses them; if not, task 6 adds them with S00.04.04's API
    (`useOneOptions`, giving `prefetch`, `take` and `spent`, and `cancelPasskey`), so that the merge keeps one copy.
    Support is checked with `browserSupportsWebAuthn()` alone, as sign-in.md's conflict 14 settles, not with the
    sketch's `getClientCapabilities()`.
  - **A `429`** on a setup call shows sign-in.md's "Too many tries. Wait 1 minute, then try again." where that call's
    failures show, as S06.01.05 leaves each screen's `429` to its own spec.
  - **CI's end-to-end run** stays on `http://localhost:5000`, and the setup tests send `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`
    through Playwright's `extraHTTPHeaders`, which the app trusts from loopback, so that the antiforgery and session
    cookies are issued `Secure`, the second of the two ways in the S00.01.04 Note on HTTPS above. Chromium keeps
    `Secure` cookies from `http://localhost`, and Identity checks a passkey's origin against the request's `Origin`
    header (the probe below), which the forwarded scheme does not change. If Chromium drops them, the run serves HTTPS
    with a development certificate instead, and the Notes say so.
  - **Setup links in the end-to-end tests** come from a fixture that runs the published app's own `admin bootstrap`
    against the run's database, with `App__Origin` the run's address, as the SSH console runs it, and a made-up
    organisation, manager and `example.org` address for each test (A35); that database is made for the run and goes
    with it.
  - **The passkey path's second sign-in** (sign-in.md's *After setup, signing in a second time works…*, design.md §8.1's
    invariant) needs `POST /api/auth/passkey`, which is S00.04.04's. If `origin/main` has it when the end-to-end test
    is written, the test signs in again with the same virtual authenticator; otherwise task 9 hands that check to
    S00.04.04 in a Note.
- Refinement probe, 12 October 2026, of ASP.NET Core Identity 10.0.3 through its decompiled IL:
  `MakePasskeyCreationOptionsAsync` keeps the ceremony's state in Identity's two-factor cookie
  (`IdentityConstants.TwoFactorUserIdScheme`, 5 minutes by default), and `PerformPasskeyAttestationAsync` reads it and
  signs that cookie out, throwing when there is none or it holds another operation, so that each options object serves
  one attempt, as sign-in.md's prefetch rule assumes; S00.04.05's 5 minutes set explicitly with no sliding (D69) change
  nothing here. `AddSignInManager` registers `IPasskeyHandler<TUser>` as scoped, and the sign-in manager takes it from
  the user manager's services. The handler's `rp.id` is `ServerDomain`, or the request's host when that is null; its
  default origin check compares the client data's origin with the request's `Origin` header; and it refuses a
  credential without user verification when that is required, and one already registered.
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.03 adds views F and G to `/setup/password` and `/setup/app` in the
  setup layout, and its `POST /api/auth/setup/password` reuses the enrolment check and completion's steps after the
  method's own (Status Active, `ActivatedAtUtc`, `EmailConfirmed`, the stamp, `auth.setup_completed`, the sign-in after
  the commit and `200 Me`); S00.04.04 signs in with the passkey stored here, under the same passkey options, and it
  and S00.04.05 put "Error: " in the page title through the same hook (sign-in.md component 1); S00.05.03 keeps
  setup outside the `/me` start-up, through its routes' `signedOutFrame` (task 9's Note to it, and the rebase Note
  below); S00.06.02 to S00.06.04's links open here; S06.01.05's limits meet the setup screen's
  `429`; and each later story that words an error code adds it to `apiErrors.ts`.
- 12 October 2026, task 1: minimal APIs mark an endpoint that reads a JSON body as accepting `application/json` only,
  and routing then passes it over for any other `Content-Type`, so the matrix's form post to `setup/start` got the
  `/api` catch-all's `404`, not the `415` that design.md §9.8 item 3 and the matrix expect (the test-only endpoints
  were routed apart from the catch-all, so none showed it). The `/api` group now removes that mark in a finally
  convention, and binding answers `415`, as `ApiGroupTests` shows at `setup/start`; every later endpoint that reads
  JSON, such as S00.04.04's and S00.04.05's sign-in, gets the same with no change of its own. Chosen here, as the
  design is silent: the scheme's name is `Enrolment`, which is also part of its tickets' Data Protection purpose, so
  neither cookie can stand in for the other, and the ticket holds the user's ID in the `NameIdentifier` claim; a body
  without `userId` or `token` gets the built-in validation's `422 validation.failed`, naming each.
- 12 October 2026, task 3: chosen here, as the design is silent. The refused passkey's `422` comes from
  `ValidationProblems.Field`, which writes the built-in validation's problem type through the problem details
  service, so it has the same `code`, `type` and camelCase key as a missing `credentialJson`; S00.04.03's wrong setup
  code (D70) can answer the same way. Identity's `InvalidOperationException` for a missing ceremony is caught around
  `PerformPasskeyAttestationAsync` alone, so one from saving or signing in still becomes a `500`. The `200 Me` helper
  is `MeEndpoint.OfUserAsync`, which reads the organisation's name by the user's own organisation, as `/me` reads it
  by the tenant; S00.04.04 and S00.04.05 reuse it. Inside the transaction completion loads the user again only while
  still Invited, and answers `401`, as the enrolment check does, when another device's completion or a deactivation
  committed after that check; one that commits after the load makes the save's concurrency check refuse it, a `500`
  with nothing committed. Completion leaves the enrolment cookie in place: while the user is Active it gets `401` at
  every setup step, and it ends with the browser session or its 30 minutes. A manager's Reset sign-in sets the user
  back to Invited (design.md §8.6), so a cookie still inside its 30 minutes then passed the enrolment check without
  the new link, as §8.1 step 3 checked only the cookie and the status. The branch review closed that gap rather than
  leave it as a question for the owner: the ticket now holds the security stamp, which a resend and a reset rotate,
  and the checks compare it (A53, its Note below).
- 12 October 2026, task 4: `origin/main` had no router or shell yet, so they are built here, with the shared pieces
  as S00.05.01's branch, not yet merged, has them, so that either merges onto the other with one version of each:
  `react-router` 8.4.0, the latest, rather than the 7 that the task first named, as S00.05.01 adds 8.4.0 and the data
  router's API used here is the same in both (`npm audit` finds nothing); and the same `src/copy/pageNames.ts`,
  `msg.pageTitle`, `strings.skipToMainContent`, `main.tsx`, `src/routes/layout.module.css`, and `AppRoute` and
  `RouteHandle` in `src/routes/routes.tsx`. Chosen where the design is silent: the root layout, `AppShell`, takes the
  name of the deepest route that declares one, and `usePageTitle` sets the title in a layout effect when that name
  changes, so that the "Error: " a screen puts in front in a `useEffect` comes after it (task 5); the header is the
  wordmark alone, as text in a 48 px row, until S00.05.01 adds the signed-in header; the three setup routes show a
  page with the `h1` "Set up your account" until tasks 5 and 6 and S00.04.03 build views D to G there; the setup
  context holds `{email, displayName}`, to which S00.04.03 adds the password, and `useSetup()` throws outside the
  layout; and the placeholder page, the old `App`, is `src/routes/PlaceholderPage.tsx` at every other address, `/`
  among them, with its own `PageStatus`. The unchanged end-to-end smoke tests pass against the published app.
- 12 October 2026, task 5: chosen here, as the design is silent. `setup/start` goes from an effect with an
  `AbortController`, so StrictMode's first run is aborted before it is sent and the link is checked once. Any fragment
  on `/setup` leaves the address, one with `u` or `t` missing too, which shows the no-link message; a link opened in
  the same tab while `/setup` is already open, which the browser takes as a new fragment only, is not checked, as the
  fragment is read once, but opened from the email in a new tab it is. While Try again runs, the status line is empty
  and the button busy, as `LoadRegion` does, so that a second failure writes its sentence into an empty region and is
  announced again; "Error: " is in front of the title while the line holds a failure sentence, the `429`'s among them,
  and not while it is empty. The `410` is told by its `code`, which `apiErrors.ts` names, and the map is checked with
  `satisfies Record<ApiErrorCode, unknown>`, so that each entry keeps its screen's shape. The token is dropped on any
  answer but one that Try again may get past. A whole-page message focuses its `h1` as it shows, the no-link message
  on a first load too, as sign-in.md's *Setup* states ask; view E, until task 6 builds it, is its `h1`, which takes
  focus when the link checks. The prefix's hook is `useErrorTitle` in `src/components/`, which `ErrorSummary` now uses,
  and `/setup/password` and `/setup/app` show the no-link message through `NoLinkPage`.
- 12 October 2026, task 6: chosen here, as the design is silent. `@simplewebauthn/browser` is 14.0.0, the latest, as
  S00.04.04's branch adds it, and `npm audit` finds nothing. View E is `ChooseMethod` in `src/routes/setup/`, which
  `SetupPage` shows once the link checks; a `401` from any of its calls goes back to `SetupPage`, which drops setup's
  memory and shows the timed-out page. `origin/main` had no passkey helpers, so `src/features/auth/passkey.ts` is added
  here as S00.04.04's branch has it, unchanged, and view E uses its `useOneOptions` and `cancelPasskey` as sign-in's
  view A does: a press while the prefetch is still on its way joins that request rather than sending another, whose
  answer would replace the first ceremony in Identity's cookie, and the options are spent once their credential is sent.
  A prefetch that fails shows nothing but for a `401`, and the press asks again. Setup needs no `isOurAbort` either: its
  only cancel of its own is as the view unmounts, after which a mounted check keeps it silent. Anything that `api()` did
  not throw comes from the prompt, an abort the view did not start among them, and shows *Passkey not set up*, as the
  `422` does; the view's own abort, as it goes, shows nothing. With no WebAuthn the notice takes the place of the alert
  line too, as nothing is sent there, and "or" stays, as the notice is a way on. On `200` the page goes to `/` with
  `replace`, and leaving the setup layout drops its memory (*Interactions* 13): dropping it first would show the no-link
  message for a render, as the router renders its navigation in a transition. The answer is typed `Me` from
  `src/api/me.ts`, as S00.05.01's and S00.04.04's branches write it, and is not put into TanStack's `['me']`
  (*Interactions* 5), which nothing reads on this branch; whichever of this story and S00.05.01 merges second puts it
  there if the shell needs it (it does, and the rebase Note below puts it there). "or" and "Email address" are
  top-level in `strings.ts`, as sign-in's views share them.
- 12 October 2026, task 7: chosen here, as the design is silent. The fixture is `invite` in `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`:
  it runs `dotnet Grow2Notes.Web.dll admin bootstrap` from `E2E_APP_DIR`, in Production, with the connection string
  and `App__Origin` from the environment, for "Sample Support Services", "Alex Sample" and
  `alex.sample.<GUID>@example.org`, and checks that the printed link opens at the tests' address; the command printed

  `Secure` and `__Host-` cookies from `http://localhost`, and the passkey, made for `rp.id` `localhost` from
  `App__Origin`, passed Identity's origin check, so the run needs no development certificate. The history is read

  fragment while `setup/start` is held back, and none is a setup page after the passkey path. "Both options still
  work" is the password link reaching `/setup/password` and Back returning to view E from memory, before the passkey
  succeeds. The app keeps nothing in `localStorage`, `sessionStorage` or IndexedDB, so the test expects each empty,
  with `document.cookie` too, and `history.state` holds no email, token or "passkey". Beyond the task's list, at little
  cost: the passkey path also checks that `GET /api/auth/me` answers `200` as the new manager and that the
  authenticator holds one discoverable passkey for the app's host, and the link check's failure state then gets past
  Try again to view E. `origin/main` (7fc98ca) has no `POST /api/auth/passkey`, so there is no second sign-in here;
  task 9 hands it to S00.04.04. Run locally against the published app on a temporary LocalDB instance, as Docker is
  not running: the 9 end-to-end tests pass, and 36 of 36 with `--repeat-each=4`; without `X-Forwarded-Proto` the link
  check fails, and with both setup `navigate` calls pushing rather than replacing, both history checks fail. CI's run
  on the pull request, which is authoritative, is checked once the branch is pushed.
- 13 October 2026, task 8: checked by hand in the machine's Google Chrome 154, driven through Playwright 1.63's `chrome`
  channel with the keyboard only, each step's screenshot looked at, with a close-up at twice the scale of each focus
  stop. The app was the published Release build, in Production, served at `https://localhost:5287` with the machine's
  trusted ASP.NET Core development certificate, so that the browser kept the `Secure` cookies with no forwarded scheme,
  with `App__Origin` that address, on a temporary LocalDB database migrated with `dotnet ef database update`; the links

  authenticator was the one DevTools' WebAuthn panel adds (`ctap2`, `internal`, resident keys and user verification),
  added through the DevTools protocol's `WebAuthn` domain, which the panel drives. Text at 200% is the browser's default
  text size at 32 px (the protocol's `Page.setFontSizes`), the body text then 36 px. All three checks hold, after the
  two fixes below.
  - **Keyboard.** At 1280 × 800 px and at 320 px with text at 200%, view E focuses its `h1` as it shows; Tab then
    reaches "Use a passkey (recommended)" and "Use a password and authenticator app", leaves the page, and comes back to
    "Skip to main content" and then the passkey button, as the wordmark is text; Shift+Tab walks the same stops back.
    Each stop shows the 3 px `#0b0c0c` ring 2 px outside it, through `:focus-visible`. Enter on the skip link focuses
    `<main>`, with no ring, the address and `history.length` as they were, and the next Tab reaches the passkey button.
    On the expired-link page the first Tab reaches the skip link.
  - **A cancelled ceremony.** A Cancel in Chrome's own passkey dialog cannot be pressed through the DevTools protocol,
    so the check took two ends that the page meets as it meets a Cancel, with a `NotAllowedError`. With the
    authenticator failing the person's verification, Enter on the passkey button gave "Your passkey was not set up. …"
    above it at once, with focus and its ring still on the button, the title "Error: Grow2Notes – Set up your account"
    and new options fetched; Tab reached the password link and Shift+Tab came back; with verification passing again,
    Enter set up the passkey and went to `/`, with no setup page left in the tab's history. With an authenticator that
    never answers (presence not simulated), Enter made the button `aria-disabled` within 250 ms and "Setting up your
    account…" by 600 ms, a second Enter sent nothing, and Chrome ended the prompt at the options' 5-minute timeout,
    300,701 ms after the press: the alert showed, the button had focus all along (a `focusout` listener heard nothing
    from the press to the end), options were fetched again, and Enter then made one discoverable passkey for `rp.id`
    `localhost` and went to `/`.
  - **Reflow.** At 320 px with text at 200% and at 1280 px, with WebAuthn and with the notice, and at 375, 639 and 640
    px: nothing scrolls sideways (the page is as wide as the window, and a sideways wheel leaves `scrollX` at 0), and no
    element or text reaches past the window but the unfocused skip link's text, which its 1 px box clips. The `<dl>` is
    a block below 40rem, each term over its value, at 320 px with text at 200% (where 40rem is 1280 px), 375 and 639 px,
    and a grid from it, at 640 and 1280 px, each value beside its term, after the terms' 115.6 px column. Below 40rem
    the buttons fill the column; from it each is as wide as its longer label with its padding and border, 300.4 px for
    the passkey button and 367.5 px for the password link, left-aligned and 48 px tall.
  - **Fixed here.** At 320 px with text at 200%, the labels' longest words ran past the buttons' edges, "(recommended)"
    9.4 px past each side of the primary button, where its white text left the fill, and "authenticator" 17.2 px past
    the secondary one's border: `place-items: center` sized a label to at least its longest word, which base.css's
    `break-word` does not shrink, so the labels' `min-inline-size: 0` alone did not stop it, and the link's text, an
    anonymous grid item, had none. `Button.module.css` now sets `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the button, which with that
    `min-inline-size` is foundations.md's rule for text in a grid, so such a word breaks inside the button, and nothing
    changed at the other widths. And the focused skip link, which wraps there, reached the window's right edge, with its
    ring's right side off screen at 325 px; it now keeps `max-inline-size: calc(100% - 2 * var(--space-2))`, as far from
    the right edge as from the left, and at 1280 px it is as before. `e2e/setup.spec.ts` gains a test of both at 320 px
    with text at 200%, with the page's width, which failed on the build without each fix (the passkey label 7.4 px past
    the button in Playwright's Chromium, the ring at 325 px) and passes with both, 5 times in 5; the 10 end-to-end tests
    pass against the published app on LocalDB. The skip link is a shared piece: whichever of this story and S00.05.01
    merges second keeps its `max-inline-size`.

- 13 October 2026, branch review before the pull request: six findings were confirmed and are fixed here. Each new
  test was shown, in a copy of the repository on a temporary LocalDB instance, to fail on the change its finding
  describes.
  - The end-to-end checks that `history.state` holds no part of the link looked only for the token as the page reads
    it, but a real token has a `+`, `/` or `=`, which the link escapes, so a page that kept the fragment as written in
    the router's state passed them. Both checks in `setup.spec.ts` now look for the user ID, the token as read, the
    token as escaped and the fragment as written, as `SetupPage.test.tsx` does. With the setup page made to keep the
    fragment (`navigate('/setup', { replace: true, state: { from: hash } })`), the old check passed and the new one
    failed.
  - Nothing tied view E's reuse of options for up to 4 minutes (`passkey.ts`) to the 5 minutes for which Identity keeps
    the ceremony in its two-factor cookie (D69), and no test let more than 2 minutes pass between the two.
    `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests` now completes a ceremony 5 minutes and 1 second after its options, which gets `422`
    and changes nothing, and then one 5 minutes after new options, which succeeds; with the two-factor cookie set to 4
    minutes, or to 6, it fails. S00.04.05, which sets the 5 minutes explicitly, has a Note on it.
  - A resend or a Reset sign-in leaves the user Invited with a new stamp, so an enrolment cookie that an older link
    started still passed the enrolment check for the rest of its 30 minutes, and whoever held it could finish setup in
    the person's place, which would end the person's new link. The task 3 Note left this to the owner where no owner
    question showed it. It is fixed instead, as the simplest way to meet design.md §8.6's "invalidates older links":
    the cookie holds the security stamp, the enrolment check compares it, and so does completion's load inside its
    transaction, so a new link sent during the passkey prompt ends that attempt too (design.md A53, with §8.1 step 3,
    §8.6 and §6.9's `401` row; the Notes above corrected). Tests: the start's ticket holds the stamp; after the stamp
    is rotated with the user still Invited, the options get `401`; and with a new link sent at the prompt, completion
    gets `401` and the user stays Invited with no passkey and no event. Without the enrolment check's comparison the
    second fails, and without the transaction's the third. S00.06.03 and S00.06.04 have a Note on it, and no question
    is left for the owner.
  - Tasks 7 and 9 were ticked, but each ends on CI's run on the pull request, which cannot have run: the branch is not
    pushed. Both are unticked until that run passes, and its ID then goes in Notes. The criteria stay ticked: on the
    LocalDB copy, `SetupStartEndpointTests` (10), `SetupPasskeyOptionsEndpointTests` (7) and
    `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests` (14), which prove them, passed with this review's tests, as did the whole .NET suite
    but `HealthEndpointsWithDatabaseStoppedTests`, which stops its database (515 tests); and the 10 end-to-end tests
    passed against the published app there, `setup.spec.ts` 21 times in 21 with `--repeat-each=3`. CI's run confirms
    them.
  - S00.03.05's third criterion, which waits on this story's tests (the carried Note above), was in no task's list, so
    it would have stayed unticked in a story that is closed. Task 9 now ticks it, with a Note in F00.03 naming the
    tests and CI's run ID.
  - sign-in.md's NVDA and VoiceOver pass, which this story leaves to "before M0 closes", had no owner. It is now part
    of this feature's manual check under *Feature checks*, by the operator by hand over views A to G together, and
    coverage.md's row for `sign-in.md › Layout and accessibility` names it; the criteria and the refinement Note above
    point there.

- From S00.04.04 (13 October 2026, its branch review; merged after this story): the passkey options that both stories
  share now set `ValidateOrigin` where `App:Origin` is set, so Identity takes a credential only from a page at

  still one. Identity applies the check to a new passkey as to a sign-in. Setup's API tests stand in for Identity's
  passkey handler, so it does not reach them, and its end-to-end test makes its passkey on a page at `App__Origin`, as
  CI's run opens it, so it passes there; a passkey made at any other origin is refused as any other that Identity
  refuses, with A52's `422`.
- 13 October 2026, tasks 7 and 9: PR #43 merged as 6bbf382, and CI's run on the pull request, 38265782762 on its head
  6876426, passed: 517 .NET tests, `SetupStartEndpointTests`, `SetupPasskeyOptionsEndpointTests` and
  `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests` among them, and the end-to-end step's `22 passed`, 7 of them in `setup.spec.ts`, with
  `fixtures.spec.ts` failing as it should. So tasks 7 and 9 are ticked, and S00.03.05's third criterion is ticked in
  F00.03, with a Note there naming the tests that show it.
- 13 October 2026, task 11, not ticked until task 10 is done: CI run 38266439018 on `main` (517 .NET tests, 339

  deploy took that run's artifact; the app deploy needed no retry, and the smoke test matched `index.html`'s

  made-up ID and token answered `410` problem+json with `code` `auth.setup_link_invalid` and `no-store`, and set no
  cookie; and the options and `POST /api/auth/setup/passkey` with no enrolment cookie each answered `401`

  after the antiforgery check, and the same checks against the build before failed. In Chromium (Playwright 1.63's,
  headless), `/setup` with no link showed "Set up your account" and its sentence, with no request to `setup/start`,
  and a made-up link showed "This link has expired", with the fragment gone from the address and from
