  Error or above from the deploy to the checks, and no `5xx` among the requests it kept: `AppRequests` left out some
  of the browser's requests, as the distro's sampler can (`Platform/Telemetry.cs`), so the logs, which are not
  sampled, are what show that nothing failed. An independent check repeated the requests, the browser check and the
  queries, and agreed on the app; this Note gives the console and the telemetry as it found them. The criteria are
  proven in CI, by the three test classes above and `setup.spec.ts`. Still to do, so tasks 10 and 11 stay unticked and
  the story `doing`: task 10, the operator's first `admin bootstrap` in test and the setup with its link, by hand, as
  `ops/break-glass.md` says. Its report then goes in a Note here, with the ticks that task 11 names: S00.03.05's first
  criterion, S00.04.01's `Set-Cookie` criterion, S00.02.02's restart criterion and S00.03.03's `IpAddress` criterion,
  each with a Note giving the run IDs of the deploy then in test.

## S00.04.03 Set up my account with a password and authenticator app

```yaml
id: S00.04.03
type: story
title: Set up my account with a password and authenticator app
status: done
points: 5
sprint: 2
depends_on: [S00.04.02, S00.05.01, S00.03.05, S00.04.01, S00.01.04, S00.01.05]
sources: [D22, D23, D42, D67, D68, D70, D71, A22, A23, A35, A53, "design.md §4.1", "design.md §5.3", "design.md §5.4", "design.md §5.9", "design.md §6.1", "design.md §6.2", "design.md §6.9", "design.md §7.5", "design.md §8.1", "design.md §8.4", "design.md §9.6", "ux/screens/sign-in.md", "ux/screens/app-shell.md", "ux/components/totp-setup.md", "ux/components/sign-in-form.md", "ux/components/form-validation.md", "ux/components/note-identity-header.md", "ux/components/microcopy.md"]
github:
azure:
```

As an invited worker or manager whose phone or browser cannot make a passkey, I want to set up a password and an
authenticator app, so that I can still sign in with MFA.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §4.1 *Setting up an account* step 4, and step 5 for the password path; §8.1 step 4 (authenticator app),
  step 5, and the *Invariant*.
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Acceptance criteria › Setup, these bullets: "Create a password
  rejects…", "The password form contains a hidden `username` field…", "View G shows the QR code…", "A wrong setup
  code…", "Back on view G…", for views F and G "A `401` from any setup call…" and "Opening `/setup`,
  `/setup/password` or `/setup/app` with nothing in memory…", and, for the password path, "Both setup paths end on
  Today…" and "After setup, signing in a second time works…" (here if S00.04.05's sign-in is on `main` when task 8
  is written, otherwise S00.04.05's; see Notes).
- sign-in.md › Acceptance criteria › Sign in, the bullet "On view F, Show reveals the password…".
- sign-in.md › Privacy and storage, its first three bullets, and › Layout and accessibility, its first two, each for
  views F and G (the second's NVDA and VoiceOver pass is this feature's check before M0 closes, under *Feature
  checks* above, as the Notes say).
- [app-shell.md](../../ux/screens/app-shell.md) component 3a, the before-main bar slot's `PageBar` and the shared
  `BackLink`, as far as views F and G use them; the shell's opened-from memory, which tells a back link when to pop,
  is S00.05.02's (see Notes).

Specific to this story:
- [x] The QR image at `/api/auth/setup/authenticator/qr.png` and the key at `GET /api/auth/setup/authenticator` hold
      the key generated when the link was sent, with issuer and label prefix Grow2Notes (design.md §8.1 step 4).
- [x] `POST /api/auth/setup/password` verifies the code, adds the password, sets `TwoFactorEnabled`, then completes as
      in S00.04.02, in one transaction.
- [x] Invariant test: each setup path end to end, then a second sign-in; no account has a password without TOTP. The
      second sign-ins need S00.04.04's and S00.04.05's endpoints, so this is ticked by whichever of this story,
      S00.04.04 and S00.04.05 makes the last of them in CI; the rest is tested here (see Notes).
- [x] No recovery codes are generated.

### Notes
- sign-in.md open question 7 assumes a wrong setup code returns `422 validation.failed` with `errors.totpCode`; confirm
  before Ready.
- "Ends on Today" means the `/` route served by S00.01.01's client-route fallback, as in S00.04.02's Notes.
- No Copy setup key button unless the owner approves one (sign-in.md open question 3).
- Owner answer, 9 October 2026: a wrong code at `POST /api/auth/setup/password` returns `422 validation.failed` with
  `errors.totpCode`, as open question 7 assumed (D70, now in design.md §6.2). There is no Copy setup key button, so
  the key never goes on the clipboard, and Show/Hide stays on Create a password only (D68). The setup copy marked (P)
  in sign-in.md, the authenticator steps included, is approved as written (D67).
- Owner answer, 11 October 2026: the API's types are written by hand, with no OpenAPI document (D71, now in design.md
  §7.5), so this story's calls name each answer's TypeScript type through the `api()` wrapper, as in S00.04.02. As
  the first field with a length rule, Create a password's 12-character minimum (`Limits.PasswordMinLength`) is the
  first limit in the SPA's `limits.ts`, and this story adds form-validation.md's .NET test, which reads that file and
  checks each limit against the server's `Limits` constants. This settles releases.md's Sprint 05 question on §7.5.
  The UX sketches name limits three ways (`GUIDE_PROMPTS_MAX`, `FLAG_REASON_MAX`, `LIMITS.reviewComment`), so this
  story picks one form for `limits.ts` and brings those sketches into line.
- From S00.05.01 (12 October 2026, its task 7): S00.05.01's fourth criterion, that both setup paths end on its `/`
  route (sign-in.md › Setup, "Both setup paths end on Today…"), is checked by whichever of S00.05.01 and this story
  merges second, and this story was not on `main` when S00.05.01 checked. So this story checks it when it is built:
  its end-to-end setup test finds, after the password path, the address `/`, Today's `<h1>` for the `today` of the
  `200 Me` that ends setup (today.md component 3), and the title "Grow2Notes – Today". That `200 Me` goes into the
  shell's `['me']` with `queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], me)`, as sign-in.md interaction 12 says, because a shell
  that stays mounted from `/setup` would otherwise keep the `401` its `['me']` got there (`staleTime: Infinity`), and
  `/` would show the signed-out frame. Once that check passes in CI and S00.04.02's has too, it ticks S00.05.01's
  fourth criterion, with a Note there giving both run IDs. "Ends on Today" in the Note above then means this route,
  not the client-route fallback.
- From S00.04.02 (13 October 2026): what this story takes from it, whose Notes have the detail. On the server: the
  enrolment cookie and its check, `EnrolmentCookie.FindInvitedUserAsync`, which each of this story's three endpoints
  calls first, answering `401` with no code when there is no valid cookie or the user is no longer Invited, as the
  passkey endpoints do; completion's steps after the method's own, as `SetupPasskeyEndpoint` takes them, which the two
  endpoints can share through one helper rather than keep two copies: one `InTransactionAsync` inside
  `ITenantContext.Use` for the user's organisation, the user loaded again while still Invited, Status Active,
  `ActivatedAtUtc`, `EmailConfirmed`, the stamp rotated and `auth.setup_completed`, then
  `SignInAsync(user, isPersistent: false)` after the commit and `200 Me` from `MeEndpoint.OfUserAsync`; and the wrong
  code's `422` with `errors.totpCode` (D70) through `ValidationProblems.Field`, as a refused passkey's (A52). In the
  tests: `SetupLinks` (`InviteAsync`, `StartSetupAsync`) and `AntiforgeryTokens` in
  `tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/`, and the end-to-end `invite` and `authenticator` fixtures and
  `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`. In the SPA: the setup layout's memory through `useSetup()`, to which this story adds the
  password; `/setup/password` and `/setup/app`, which show `NoLinkPage` until this story puts views F and G there,
  keeping the no-link message for a page opened with nothing in memory; view E's link to `/setup/password`; the
  timed-out page for a `401` (`SetupMessage` with `strings.setup.timedOut`); `useErrorTitle` for "Error: " in the
  title; `msg.notSetUp` and `strings.setup.tooManyTries` for a failed request and a `429`; `Me` from
  `src/api/me.ts`; and, on `200`, `queryClient.setQueryData(meQuery.queryKey, me)`, as the S00.05.01 Note above asks,
  then `navigate('/', { replace: true })`, as leaving the setup layout drops its memory (sign-in.md *Interactions*
  13). The setup routes' `handle` is `setupHandle` in `routes.tsx`, whose `signedOutFrame` keeps them outside the
  shell's wait for `/me`.
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 after S00.04.02, as its plan allows
  (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and S00.04.05
  (5 points), it stays at 5, at the top of that size. Its eleven tasks add three endpoints over Identity, one of them
  finishing setup through the passkey path's completion, and QRCoder for the QR code; two views that are forms, with
  a password field that shows and hides, a QR code, a setup key that loads and a code step with six ways to fail;
  the shell's before-main bar and back link, built here as their first user; `limits.ts` with its .NET test; and an
  end-to-end test that reads the key from the page and computes codes from it. That is about as much as Sign in with
  email, password and code, the 5-point reference, with its two steps over two endpoints, and as S00.04.02, also 5:
  the enrolment cookie, the completion and the setup frame are built already and no step needs the operator, but the
  views have more states, and the parts that sign-in shares (the code field and its check on both sides, the password
  field) and that later screens share (the bar and the back link) are built here. It is not clearly an 8 to split.
  `depends_on` now names S00.04.02, whose enrolment cookie, completion, setup layout, view E and end-to-end fixtures it
  builds on; S00.05.01, whose shell, `#page-bar` slot, `['me']` query and Today it ends on; S00.03.05, whose invite
  core stores the key that the views show; S00.04.01, whose sign-in manager and `Me` completion uses; and S00.01.04
  and S00.01.05, whose `api()` wrapper, form parts and copy module the views use. All are done but S00.04.02,
  S00.05.01 and S00.01.05, earlier in this sprint (merged in PRs #43, #41 and #37, their checks after the deploy to
  come). `sources` adds D22, D67, D68, D70, D71, A35 and A53, which settle its storage, wording, extras, API details
  and setup window; design.md §5.4, §5.9, §6.1, §6.9, §7.5, §8.4 and §9.6;
  app-shell.md and note-identity-header.md
  for the bar and the back link; form-validation.md for `limits.ts`; and microcopy.md for the words. The linked
  criteria add the *Setup* bullets on a `401` and on a page with nothing in memory, the *Privacy and storage* and
  *Layout and accessibility* bullets for views F and G, and app-shell.md component 3a; the invariant criterion says
  who ticks it. No owner question is open for it (sprint-02.md, Definition of Ready check), and it adds no (P)
  string. Nothing checks for the parent company's name (D72), and nothing is done by the operator: a password setup
  in test on a real phone and a laptop, with a real authenticator app, is this feature's check (*Feature checks*), as
  S00.04.04 left its sign-in on a real device there; and so is sign-in.md's NVDA and VoiceOver pass over views F and
  G, which the stories leave to that check, and which is also the Definition of Done's screen-reader spot check for
  these two forms. The story therefore closes with that Definition of Done line handed on to the feature check, and
  its closing pull request records it as handed on, not as done. Tasks 5 to 7 test the forms' names, descriptions,
  live lines and focus, task 8 runs axe on them, and task 9 is the keyboard pass.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **The key and its QR code.** Both `GET` endpoints read the key that the invite stored (`GetAuthenticatorKeyAsync`)
    and never make a new one, so neither changes the security stamp, and a person who opens the link again sees the
    same key (design.md §8.1 step 1). The URI is built in one place, which both use: §8.1's
    `otpauth://totp/Grow2Notes:<email>?secret=<key>&issuer=Grow2Notes&digits=6`, with the email escaped by
    `Uri.EscapeDataString` and nothing more, as Identity checks SHA-1 codes of 30-second steps, the apps' defaults.
    The image is QRCoder's `PngByteQRCode`, which needs no `System.Drawing` on Linux App Service, at error correction
    level M, with its 4-module quiet zone and the fewest pixels per module that make it at least 200 px across
    (totp-setup.md *Implementation notes*). `GET /api/auth/setup/authenticator` answers
    `{sharedKey, authenticatorUri}`, as §6.2 gives it; the SPA's type, written by hand (D71), names `sharedKey` only,
    as no screen shows the URI (totp-setup.md).
  - **The code's check** is one helper in `Features/Auth/`, which S00.04.05's code step can share, so that both read a
    code alike, as the SPA's `code.ts` does: NFKC, then whitespace, hyphens, dashes and minus signs removed, then
    exactly six ASCII digits, so that a badly formed code never reaches Identity. Setup verifies the code with
    `UserManager.VerifyTwoFactorTokenAsync` and the authenticator provider, as Identity's own template does, so a
    wrong setup code counts towards no lockout (totp-setup.md *Where it's used* 1; the rate limiter, S06.01.05, bounds
    the guesses), and it writes no audit event, as design.md §5.4's `auth.mfa_failed` is a sign-in's. A badly formed
    code and a wrong one get the same `422` with `errors.totpCode` (D70), through `ValidationProblems.Field`, as a
    refused passkey's `422` is made (A52). The code is checked before the transaction, as it only reads the key.
  - **The password's check** is Identity's, with design.md §8.4's password options set here, as this is the first
    story that adds a password: `RequiredLength` from `Limits.PasswordMinLength`, no composition rules, and
    Identity's default of one unique character; S00.04.05 adds the lockout options beside them. Identity's defaults
    want a digit, a capital, a lowercase letter and a symbol, so the tests set up with 12 lowercase letters and are
    refused with their first 11, which pins `RequiredLength` at 12 and shows that no composition rule is left on. The
    endpoint runs Identity's password validators itself, after the code's check and before the transaction, as
    `UserManager`'s own check is protected, and a password that they refuse gets `422 validation.failed` with
    `errors.password` (§6.1) and changes nothing. In the transaction `AddPasswordAsync` checks it again, so a refusal
    there is only a fault or another request's change, which throws, as the passkey path's does (the review Note
    below). A password of only whitespace never reaches Identity: the body's `[Required]` refuses it as it refuses an
    empty one, before the endpoint runs (*The body* below), as `RequiredAttribute` takes a string for which
    `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` is true as missing; Identity would refuse it too, as `PasswordTooShort` (the probes
    below). A check of `value.length` alone would pass it, so view F says "Enter a password" when
    `value.trim() === ''`, as for an empty field. JavaScript's `trim()` removes every character that
    `IsNullOrWhiteSpace` counts, tabs, no-break spaces and the other Unicode spaces among them, but U+0085, and also
    U+FEFF, which it does not count; no keyboard types either, so the two sides agree for any password a person enters
    (form-validation.md's "Not blank"), and the `422` comes only if they drift. With these options Identity's one
    refusal is `PasswordTooShort`, so F shows "Password must be 12 characters or more" for it, with focus on its
    field: the "that message" of sign-in.md *Password rejected by server*, in F's own words, as the server's text is
    not for the screen, and approved with sign-in.md's other (P) strings (D67).
  - **Completion** is the passkey path's (the S00.04.02 Note above), moved into one helper that both endpoints call
    with the method's own step: inside the `Use` block and the transaction, once the user is loaded again while still
    Invited with the cookie's stamp, the password path adds the password and sets `TwoFactorEnabled`, then the shared
    steps follow. `AddPasswordAsync` and `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync` rotate the stamp as well, which changes nothing,
    as completion rotates it again. Each endpoint checks its method before the transaction, so the method's step
    throws for any refusal (`SetupCompletion.ThrowIfRefused`), which rolls the transaction back.
  - **The body** of `POST /api/auth/setup/password` binds `password` and `totpCode`, both `[Required]`, so a body
    without one, or with one that is empty or only whitespace, gets the built-in validation's
    `422 validation.failed`, naming it, before the endpoint runs, and so before the enrolment check: such a body gets
    that `422` even with no enrolment cookie, not the `401` of the endpoint's first step. `RequiredAttribute` takes a
    string as missing when `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` is true, the set that the password's check above compares with
    JavaScript's `trim()`. The password is never trimmed.
  - **Codes in the tests.** Identity's authenticator provider makes no codes, so the .NET tests compute them with a
    small RFC 6238 helper in `tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/` (HMAC-SHA1 of the 30-second step, six digits, from
    the base32 key), and the end-to-end tests with one in `tests/e2e/` on `node:crypto`; S00.04.05 needs both, so
    whichever story comes first adds them. Both read the real clock, as Identity checks a code against the system
    clock, not `TimeProvider` (the probe below), so a test on `TestClock` still computes its codes from the real time.
    A wrong code is the right key's code for a step ten minutes away, well outside the steps either side of now that
    Identity accepts.
  - **The key is fetched as view G shows**, not from view E's link as sign-in.md *Interactions* 9 has it: with
    `gcTime: 0`, which drops the key from memory once no view uses it, a fetch made before G with nothing observing it
    would be dropped at once, and F observing it would keep the key in memory after G unmounts, against sign-in.md's
    second *Privacy and storage* bullet. The answer is small, so G shows "Loading…" only on a slow connection. A
    failure shows in a `LoadRegion` (S00.01.04): "The setup key did not load: … Try again.", for a `429` too, as no
    button sent the request for "Too many tries" to show above; a `401` gives the timed-out page.
  - **The setup key's markup.** The key is a `<dl>`, so that it is named by its term: the `<dt>` "Setup key", and a
    `<dd>` with `translate="no"` and `user-select: all` holding the groups of four, each a `<span>` that is an inline
    block with a margin after it. totp-setup.md's CSS sketch made the key a flex box, but flex items are blocks, which
    copy with a line break between groups in Chromium and WebKit (probed in Playwright on 13 October 2026: flex groups
    copy as "GROW", "TWON" and "OTES" on lines of their own, inline blocks as "GROWTWONOTES"), against sign-in.md's
    criterion that the key "copies with no spaces"; inline blocks still wrap between groups and never inside one.
    This story brings that sketch into line as it builds the key, so it is not in task 10's list. The key's size is
    `--font-size-h2`: tokens.css has no 1.25rem, the sketch's size, which falls between body text's 1.125rem and
    `--font-size-h2`'s 1.375rem, and the larger keeps the key apart from the text around it, as the sketch meant. The
    `LoadRegion` holds only what the request brings, the term, the key and its note, so step 2's lead-in ("Using this
    phone? …") shows with the QR code while the key loads or after it failed. A key that did not load puts no
    "Error: " in the title, as microcopy.md and app-shell.md keep that for a failed submit, which this is not.
  - **The end of the password path.** F and G are each pushed, so that Back on G returns to F (sign-in.md); but then
    `navigate('/', { replace: true })` from G would leave F's entry behind Today, where Back would show the no-link
    message, against "Back from Today does not return to a setup or sign-in step". So on `200 Me` G puts the `Me` into
    `['me']`, marks setup finished in its memory and goes back two entries, to `/setup`'s, which F and G always follow
    while setup's memory lives; there the setup layout, finding setup finished, replaces the entry with `/` before
    anything shows. The tab then holds one entry for all of setup, as on the passkey path, and the two later ones stay
    as Forward's, where F shows the no-link message, as leaving the layout has dropped its memory.
  - **The code step's edges** (task 7). A `422` with `errors.password` goes back one entry to F, which learns of it from
    setup's memory, never router state (sign-in.md *Interactions* 7): the password is marked refused until F takes one
    again, and F then shows its message on the field, which takes focus in place of the `h1`. A `200 Me` that comes
    after G has gone, as when the back link was pressed while it was on its way, still goes into `['me']` and marks
    setup finished, but moves nobody, as going back two entries from elsewhere would leave the wrong ones: the person
    stays where they went, now signed in, and `/setup`'s entry becomes Today's when they reach it. The code's digits
    are `--font-size-h2`, 22 px on a phone and 24 px from 40rem, as tokens.css has no size for sign-in.md's 24 px,
    and the field is at most as wide as its column, so that its `10ch` at that size never runs past a 320 px screen
    with text at 200% inside step 3's list.
  - **The back links pop** when the previous entry is their destination, which app-shell.md component 3a learns from
    the shell's opened-from memory, S00.05.02's. Until that exists, `BackLink` takes it from its page, and F and G say
    so always, as while setup's memory lives F's entry always follows `/setup`'s and G's F's; S00.05.02's memory then
    takes over, as setup's links are in-app pushes that it records (task 10's Note to it). `PageBar` renders into the
    shell's `#page-bar` slot, which S00.05.01 left empty for its first user (F00.05's Notes), in the page's column.
    The shell's focus on a screen change is S00.05.02's too, so F and G focus their `h1` as they show, as setup's
    other views do.
  - **The shared parts' place and words.** `PasswordInput`, `OneTimeCodeField` and the code's check (`code.ts`) go in
    `src/features/auth/`, beside `passkey.ts` and S00.04.04's sign-in view, as sign-in's views A to C use them too
    (sign-in.md components 7 and 10). That departs from the *Files* lines of both UX specs, which disagree with each
    other: totp-setup.md puts the code field and `code.ts` in `src/auth/`, and sign-in-form.md puts `PasswordInput`
    and the code field, which it calls `CodeInput`, in `components/form/`. The SPA has neither folder: what sign-in
    and setup share is in `src/features/auth/` (`passkey.ts`, and S00.04.04's view on PR #42), and `src/components/`
    holds the parts that every feature uses (`Button`, `FieldGroup`, `LoadRegion`), while these three serve sign-in
    and setup only. Views F and G, with G's parts, go in `src/routes/setup/` beside S00.04.02's views, not in
    sign-in-form.md's `features/auth/setup/`; `PageBar` and `BackLink` go in `src/components/`, as later screens use
    them. Task 10 brings both specs' *Files* lines into line, with sign-in-form.md's `CodeInput` renamed
    `OneTimeCodeField`, the name sign-in.md and totp-setup.md give it. S00.04.05 builds its views from the same specs
    at the same time, and copies at different paths would not conflict in git, so after the last rebase this story
    looks for a second password field, code field or code check, under any path or name, in the SPA and on the
    server, and keeps one (task 10). The code field's words go in `strings.oneTimeCode` (its label, which is also its
    empty field's message, and "The code must be 6 digits"), with each view's hint in its own group, and the toggle's
    in `strings.passwordToggle`, so that S00.04.05 finds them. The 12 in "Use at least 12 characters." and "Password
    must be 12 characters or more" comes from `limits.ts` through `msg`, so that the words cannot drift from the rule.
  - **`limits.ts`** is the SPA's `src/limits.ts`: one `limits` object, `as const`, each key the camelCase name of the
    `Limits` constant it mirrors (`passwordMinLength` for `PasswordMinLength`), holding only the limits the SPA uses,
    each added by the story that first checks it. The .NET test reads it as text through `Repository.Root()` and
    fails on a value that differs, a key with no `Limits` constant and a file it reads no limit from. The UX sketches
    take that one form (task 3), as the Note of 11 October 2026 above says.
- Refinement probe, 12 October 2026, of Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Core 10.0.12, which the app's Identity package
  brings: its `PasswordValidator`, with design.md §8.4's options, refuses a password of twelve spaces as
  `PasswordTooShort` and accepts twelve other characters, accented ones among them; and the assembly references no
  `TimeProvider`, only the system clock's `UtcNow`, so its `Rfc6238AuthenticationService` checks a code against the
  real time. Probed again on 13 October 2026, with the same options: twelve tabs, no-break spaces (U+00A0), em spaces
  (U+2003), ideographic spaces (U+3000) or U+0085 are refused as `PasswordTooShort` too, and twelve U+FEFF accepted,
  as `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` has it; twelve lowercase letters, accented or all the same, are accepted, and eleven
  refused.
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.05 signs password users in with the password and key set here,
  through the authenticator provider that `AddDefaultTokenProviders()` registers here, and reuses `PasswordInput`
  (with no toggle and `current-password`, D68), `OneTimeCodeField`, `code.ts`, the server's code check, both test
  suites' code helpers and an account made through this path in its end-to-end test, and, if task 8 could not, signs
  in a second time in `setup.spec.ts`'s password path (task 10's Note to it); S00.05.02 builds the opened-from memory
  that tells `BackLink` when to pop; S00.04.08's in-place sign-in uses `OneTimeCodeField` with a plain label;
  S00.06.03 and S00.06.04's new links open these views, which show whatever key the user then holds; and each later
  story that checks a length adds its limit to `limits.ts`.
- 13 October 2026, task 9: checked by hand in the machine's Google Chrome 154, driven through Playwright 1.63's `chrome`
  channel with the keyboard only, in a new profile each time, each step's screenshot looked at, with a close-up at twice
  the scale of each focus stop. The app was this branch's published Release build (48e831c), in Production, served at
  `https://localhost:5293` with the machine's trusted ASP.NET Core development certificate, with `App__Origin` that
  address, on a temporary LocalDB database migrated with `dotnet ef database update`; the links came from its
  `admin bootstrap`, run for each pass, each printing the link alone in about 1.2 s. Text at 200% is Chrome's own font
  size setting at 32 px, written into the profile (`webkit.webprefs.default_font_size`), the body text then 36 px, not
  the DevTools protocol's `Page.setFontSizes` that S00.04.02's check used, which a full-page screenshot resets (probed).
  Every check holds, and nothing needed changing.
  - **Keyboard.** At 1280 × 800 px and at 320 px with text at 200%, F and G each focus their `h1` as they show. On F,
    Shift+Tab reaches the back link "Set up your account", in the bar before `<main>`, then Tab the field, Show (named
    "Show password") and Continue, then leaves the page, and Shift+Tab comes back to Continue; on G, the back link
    "Create a password", the code field and Finish setup, the QR code and the key being no stops. Each stop shows the
    3 px `#0b0c0c` ring 2 px outside it, through `:focus-visible`. Enter in F's field, empty, gave "Enter a password"
    and, with 11 letters, "Password must be 12 characters or more", each on the field, which kept focus, with "Error: "
    in the title; with 12 letters it went to G, having sent no request. Enter in G's field, empty or with "12345", gave
    the field's message with focus on it and sent nothing. Enter on Show, then Space on Hide, each left focus on the
    toggle, named "Hide password" and then "Show password", its polite line, empty until then, saying "Your password is
    visible" and then "Your password is hidden". A code for a step ten minutes away, by Enter in the field, got `422`
    and "That code did not work. …" on the emptied field, which had focus, with "Error: " in the title. Enter on the
    back link then returned to F, adding no history entry, with the password still in the field; Enter in the field
    reached G again, and the right code ended at `/` with the title "Grow2Notes – Today".
  - **Reflow.** At 1280 × 800 px and at 320 px with text at 200%, and also at 1280 and 375 px with text at 200% and at
    640, 639, 375 and 320 px, on F with and without its error and on G: nothing scrolls sideways (the page is as wide as
    the window, and scrolling it sideways leaves `scrollX` at 0), and nothing reaches past the window but the unfocused
    skip link's text, which its 1 px box clips. Show/Hide sits beside the field at every width with text at 100%, at
    320 px with F's error bar too (the field then 173.7 px wide), and at 1280 px with text at 200%, and wraps under it
    at 375 and 320 px with text at 200%. The QR code shows at 200 × 200 px at every size, inside the window, with the
    key under it: on one line at 1280, 640 and 639 px and at 1280 px with text at 200%, in two lines of groups at 375
    and 320 px, two groups to a line at 375 px with text at 200%, and one to a line at 320 px with text at 200%, where
    the key is 44 px. Each group stays on one line inside the key's box, so the key wraps between groups only.
  - **Dark mode.** With Chrome's automatic dark mode for web contents on, as its flag turns on the feature
    `WebContentsForceDark`, at the flag's default at 1280 and 375 px, and at 1280 px with the feature's parameters for
    CIELAB inversion with selective image inversion, and for the image classifier by transparency and number of colours,
    with and without RGB inversion, the page beside the QR code drew as rgb(18, 18, 18), where its stylesheet's
    background is white, while the QR code matched the server's PNG, drawn at the same size, in every pixel, its quiet
    zone white: it is not inverted. A control with the panel's `color-scheme: light only` taken out of the stylesheet
    matched in every pixel too, so Chrome 154 leaves this image as it is, and the rule is there for a browser whose
    darkening might invert it, such as Samsung Internet, which sign-in.md names and the feature check's phone may use.
  - Beyond the task's list: once setup ended on Today, focus was on `<body>`, not on Today's `h1` as sign-in.md's *Where
    focus goes after actions* has it for *Success*. That is the app-wide rule on a screen change, which S00.05.02 builds
    ("I want each new screen to put focus on its heading"), so nothing changed here.
- 13 October 2026, task 10: rebased onto `origin/main` (9125e86), which had S00.04.04's passkey sign-in (PR #42) and
  S00.04.06's sign-out (PR #44), with each shared piece kept once. Five files conflicted. sprint-02.md keeps this
  story's *Plan* row, pull-in paragraph, Definition of Ready column and pull-in line beside S00.04.04's and S00.04.06's.
  `AppShell.tsx` keeps the `#page-bar` slot in state, with `PageBarContext` around `<main>` (task 4), and S00.04.04's
  view A in `<main>` for a `401`, with its focus move after a sign-in and its "Sign in" title. `messages.ts` keeps view
  F's two messages beside S00.04.06's `notSignedOut`. tests/README.md keeps task 8's paragraph on how a test gets a
  code, and `main`'s on `X-Forwarded-Proto`, as `playwright.config.ts` now sends that header for every test, so
  `setup.spec.ts` has no `test.use` of its own. `setup.spec.ts` takes `boxOf` from `main`'s `layout.ts` and keeps task
  8's `tabHistory`, which replaced `historyUrls` to give the index of the entry shown too, its DevTools session still
  left attached, as S00.04.04 found it must be. `Program.cs`, `Features/Auth/`, `EndpointMatrix.cs` and `strings.ts`
  merged with no conflict, and the passkey options block is still one, with S00.04.04's `ValidateOrigin`.
  - Then the SPA, the server and the tests were searched, under any path or name, for a password field

- 13 October 2026, whole-branch review: five findings were confirmed and are fixed here. Each new test was shown, in a
  copy of the repository on a temporary LocalDB instance, to fail on the change its finding describes.
  - Two completions from one link can overlap, as a phone and a laptop, or two tabs, can each start setup with it. When
    one commits after the other's transaction has loaded the user, the other's `AddPasswordAsync` saves through
    Identity's store, whose check of `ConcurrencyStamp` then matches no row, so it returns `ConcurrencyFailure`. The
    endpoint took every refusal of `AddPasswordAsync` for a refused password, so that device got `422` with
    `errors.password`, and view F said "Password must be 12 characters or more" of a valid password, its next step
    getting the timed-out page. The endpoint now runs Identity's password validators itself, after the code's check
    and before the transaction, and only their refusal gets that `422`; in the transaction a refusal of
    `AddPasswordAsync` throws, as the passkey path's does, so that device gets `500`, which view G shows as "Not set
    up: something went wrong. Try again.", and nothing of its attempt commits (the Notes above). As neither endpoint's
    step now returns a refusal, completion's helper takes a step that throws for one, and returns the user's `Me`, or
    nothing for the `401`. `SetupPasswordEndpointTests` now holds back the first device's first save in completion's
    transaction, completes setup from a second client started with the same link, and then lets the first go: it gets
    `500` problem details and no session, and the user is Active, with the password, two-factor sign-in and one
    `auth.setup_completed`. With the old endpoint it got `422`. design.md is silent on two completions at once, so
    this changes no design and needs no A-number.
  - The QR code's `image/png` is a new kind of response, which tests/README.md asks to join `SecurityHeadersTests`,
    and F06.01's Notes name this story for it, but it had not joined. A case there now starts setup at
    `https://grow2notes.example`, as a client of that test, and finds design.md §9.7's six headers once each, with
    their values, on the image's `200`; task 1 names it. With the headers middleware made to leave a PNG without them,
    it failed, and the test's other cases passed.
  - Task 8 was ticked, but it ends on CI's run on the pull request, which cannot have run, as the branch is not
    pushed. It is unticked until that run passes, and the run's ID then goes in these Notes, as task 10's does.
  - sprint-02.md still said that 17 is left for this story, under its own item 17. It now says that 18 is left for
    S00.04.05.
  - The Notes and sign-in.md said that Identity refuses a password of only whitespace, which put that refusal after
    the enrolment check, but the body's `[Required]` refuses it first, before the endpoint runs, so it gets `422` with
    `errors.password` even with no enrolment cookie. *The password's check* and *The body* above, and sign-in.md's
    *Create a password, empty*, now say so. JavaScript's `trim()` still agrees with the server, as `RequiredAttribute`
    counts the characters that `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` counts.
  - After the fixes, the .NET build with warnings as errors passed, and on the LocalDB copy, removed afterwards, the
    whole .NET suite passed but `HealthEndpointsWithDatabaseStoppedTests`, which stops its database (623 of 624), and
    the two new tests passed 8 times in 8. Nothing in the SPA changed. CI's run on the pull request confirms them.
- 13 October 2026, task 11: PR #45 merged as e92ff93; CI's run on the pull request, 38306258899 on its head 9ca4e87,
  passed (624 .NET tests, 496 Vitest tests in 31 files and the end-to-end step's `44 passed`, with `setup.spec.ts`'s
  password path ending on Today, view F's refusals and Show/Hide, and F and G at 320 px with text at 200%), so tasks 8
  and 10 are ticked, and S00.05.01's fourth criterion, with a Note there. CI run 38306808797 on `main` (the same
  counts) and Deploy run 38307378063 succeeded, every step green: the app deploy needed no retry, and the smoke test
  matched `index.html`'s `Last-Modified` on its first attempt, `/healthz` answered `Healthy`, and the antiforgery
  check got `204` with the token header and the `__Host-grow2notes-xsrf` cookie with its four attributes. Requests
  that change nothing, made after the deploy: `/setup/password` and `/setup/app` served this build's `index.html`,
  byte for byte, whose hashed script, `/assets/index-Dp0cCPKu.js`, answered `200` and holds the QR code's path and
  `/setup/app`, which the build before did not; `GET /api/auth/setup/authenticator` and
  `GET /api/auth/setup/authenticator/qr.png` with no enrolment cookie each answered `401` problem+json with
  `no-store`; and after `GET /api/auth/antiforgery`, `POST /api/auth/setup/password` with a made-up body and no
  enrolment cookie answered the same. As controls, that `POST` without the token got `400`, and with an empty
  password `422` with `errors.password`. In Chromium (Playwright 1.63's, headless), both pages with nothing in memory
  showed the no-link message, with focus on its `h1`, no field, no setup request, nothing stored and no console error
  but the shell's expected `401`. Application Insights had no exception, no `5xx` and no trace at Error or above from
  the deploy to the checks. An independent check repeated the requests, the browser check and the queries, and
  agreed. The criteria are proven in CI: the first by `SetupAuthenticatorEndpointsTests`, the second and fourth by
  `SetupPasswordEndpointTests`, and the third as the next Note says. A setup on this path in test on a real phone and
  a laptop, and the NVDA and VoiceOver pass over views F and G, are F00.04's feature check, by the operator.
- 13 October 2026, the third criterion: both setup paths now sign in a second time in CI, the passkey path's in CI's
  run 38271751219 on PR #42 (S00.04.04) and the password path's, the last of the two, in 38313105836 on PR #46
  (S00.04.05), passing again on `main` in 38313556630; and no account gets a password without two-factor sign-in
  (`SetupPasswordEndpointTests`, and S00.04.05's fifth criterion). So the third criterion is ticked, and the story is
  done.

      exception and no `5xx` from the deploy to the checks; record the run IDs in Notes, and that the criteria are
      proven in CI, a setup on this path in test on a real phone and a laptop being F00.04's feature check

---

## S00.04.04 Sign in with a passkey

```yaml
id: S00.04.04
type: story
title: Sign in with a passkey
status: done
points: 5
sprint: 2
depends_on: [S00.04.01, S00.03.03, S00.01.03, S00.01.04]
sources: [D23, A22, A25, A28, "design.md §4.1", "design.md §5.4", "design.md §5.9", "design.md §6.1", "design.md §6.2", "design.md §7.3", "design.md §7.5", "design.md §8.2", "design.md §8.4", "design.md §9.5", "design.md §9.6", "design.md §9.7", "design.md §9.8", "design.md §9.9", "design.md §14 M0", "ux/screens/sign-in.md", "ux/screens/app-shell.md", "ux/components/app-shell-nav.md", "ux/components/form-validation.md", "ux/components/microcopy.md", "ux/components/passkey-flows.md", "ux/components/primary-actions.md"]
github:
azure:
```

As a worker, I want to sign in with my passkey in one tap, so that I can start writing notes quickly at the start of a
shift.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §4.1 *Signing in*; §8.2 *Passkey*; §6.2's rows for `POST /api/auth/passkey/options` and
  `POST /api/auth/passkey`; §8.4's `IdentityPasskeyOptions`.
- [design.md §14 M0](../../design.md#m0-skeleton-hosting-sign-in) *Done when*, "Successful and failed sign-ins appear
  in the audit log" (passkey sign-ins).
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Acceptance criteria › Sign in, these bullets, as far as view A's passkey
  part goes: "With no session, any URL shows view A…", "Dismissing the passkey prompt…", "With
  `window.PublicKeyCredential` removed…", "A second tap while busy…" and "The busy label appears only if…". Their
  halves for the password sign-in are S00.04.05's (task 9): the route that renders after a password sign-in, the
  password form that still works without WebAuthn, and the busy guard and 400 ms busy label of Continue and of the
  code step's "Sign in".
- sign-in.md *Components* rows 1 to 4, 9 and 18, as far as view A's passkey part goes; its *States* › Sign in rows
  Default, Already signed in, Passkeys unavailable, Busy, Sign-in failed, Passkey prompt dismissed, Passkey other
  client error, No connection, Server error and Success, fresh load, for the passkey button; and *Interactions and
  focus* steps 1, 2 and 5. [app-shell.md](../../ux/screens/app-shell.md) component 4's `401` row and component 7's
  fresh-load case.

Specific to this story:
- [x] `POST /api/auth/passkey/options` returns discoverable-credential options (no email needed) for the
      environment's own domain; `POST /api/auth/passkey` signs in with user verification required.
- [x] A success writes `auth.signin_succeeded`; a failure on a known account writes `auth.signin_failed`; an unknown
      credential is only counted in telemetry.
- [x] Given a deactivated person's passkey, when they try to sign in, then the generic "Sign-in failed…" message, and
      no session.
- [x] Given no passkey sign-in underway, or one whose options are more than 5 minutes old, when a credential is sent,
      then it is a failed sign-in like any other: the same `401` and no session, never a `500`.
- [x] A test records whether Identity's lockout blocks `PasskeySignInAsync` and whether passkey failures count, and the
      result goes to the owner (sign-in.md open question 8).

### Notes
- From S00.04.01 (9 October 2026): on ASP.NET Core 10.0.0 to 10.0.3, `SignInManager.PasskeySignInAsync` does not call
  `PreSignInCheck`, so neither `Grow2NotesSignInManager`'s `CanSignInAsync` (Status Active) nor the lockout check runs
  for a passkey; 10.0.4 added the call (dotnet/aspnetcore PR #65316). The app is framework-dependent on App Service's
  `DOTNETCORE|10.0`, which runs the current patch, and CI installs the latest 10.0 SDK and its runtime, but a
  developer machine still on 10.0.3, as S00.04.01's was, would let a deactivated person's passkey sign in. On a
  current runtime, this story's criterion for a deactivated person's passkey passes and its lockout test finds that
  lockout blocks a passkey; on 10.0.3, neither would. So it checks which runtime its tests run on, and either relies on
  10.0.4 or later, saying so, or adds a Status check where the passkey sign-in ends.
- From S00.04.02 (13 October 2026, its task 9): `origin/main` (7fc98ca) had no `POST /api/auth/passkey` when
  S00.04.02's end-to-end test was written, so its passkey path does not sign in a second time, and sign-in.md's Setup
  bullet "After setup, signing in a second time works…", for the passkey path, is this story's (S00.04.02's linked
  criteria). Whichever of the two stories merges second extends the passkey path in `tests/e2e/setup.spec.ts`: after
  setup ends at `/`, it clears the cookies, signs in again with the passkey that the same virtual authenticator made,
  under the passkey options that setup uses (design.md §8.4), and finds `GET /api/auth/me` answering `200` as the new
  manager, and a Note here gives the ID of the CI run that shows it.
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the
  reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and S00.04.05 (5 points), it is re-sized from 3 to 5. It is
  the first sign-in story in the backlog order, so besides its two endpoints over Identity, two audit events and a
  telemetry count, it builds what the password sign-in then extends: the start-up that shows sign-in for a `401` from
  `/api/auth/me`, and view A in the signed-out shell. Its API tests also need passkey assertions signed in .NET, as
  no browser runs there, and its end-to-end test drives Chromium's virtual authenticator. With a screen, two endpoints
  over Identity and a lockout test, it has the 5-point reference's shape: that story has one more step on screen and
  two more audit events, but needs none of the WebAuthn test machinery. It is clearly more than S00.04.01, a 3 with no
  screen and one small endpoint. `depends_on` now names the done stories whose code its tasks build on: S00.04.01
  (`Grow2NotesSignInManager`, `Me`, the session cookie and its test fixtures), S00.03.03 (the audit writer),
  S00.01.03 (`Button` and `PageStatus`) and S00.01.04 (`api()`, `ErrorSummary`, `RequestAlert` and `PageHeading`).
  It does not depend on S00.04.02, though it follows it in the backlog order: its API tests store a passkey through
  `UserManager`, as setup stores one, and its end-to-end test uses a passkey that no account holds (the Note on the
  end-to-end test below). The linked sign-in.md bullets were narrowed to what it builds (the next Note); sign-in.md's
  components, states and steps for the passkey button, §6.2's two rows and §8.4's passkey options were linked; the
  first criterion now names the environment's domain and the third that no session starts; and a fourth was added for
  a credential sent with no ceremony underway.
- The sign-in view here is view A's passkey part only: the error summary, the `<h1>` "Sign in" and its hint, the
  alert line, the passkey button or the notice that replaces it, and the help line. The "or", the email and password
  form, Continue and the code step are S00.04.05's, which links every Sign in bullet not listed here, so it now takes
  the three that need the form: "Tab order on A…", "The passkey button is `type="button"`…" and "Only "Sign in with a
  passkey" (A) and "Sign in" (B)…". Four of the bullets linked here also have a half for the password sign-in, which
  S00.04.05 takes too (task 9). This story still builds the button as a primary `type="button"` (sign-in.md
  component 4).
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **The account behind a credential.** `POST /api/auth/passkey` reads the credential's ID from `credentialJson` and
    finds its account with `UserManager.FindByPasskeyIdAsync` before signing in, so it sets the tenant from the loaded
    user (design.md §5.9 item 1) and audits a failure against that account. A credential whose ID matches no passkey,
    or that has no ID it can read, is an unknown credential: one log entry, no audit row (§5.4: known accounts only)
    and `401`, without calling `PasskeySignInAsync`.
  - **The count in telemetry** is one log entry at Information from the app's namespace, which appsettings.json sends
    to Application Insights, with a fixed message, such as "Passkey sign-in with an unknown credential", and no
    values: no credential ID, user handle or address (design.md §9.5). It is counted in Log Analytics by its message.
    S00.04.05 counts an unknown email the same way.
  - **The audit rows** name the person as both `ActorUserId` and the `User` entity, with no `Details`: the event, the
    person and the time say what happened, and each person has one sign-in method (A22). They are written after
    Identity's own save of the passkey's sign count, with no transaction, as design.md §5.9 lists none for a sign-in.
    If the write fails, the exception handler's `500` clears the response's headers, the session cookie's
    `Set-Cookie` among them, so no session starts without its event.
  - **A ceremony that is missing or too old.** Identity keeps a passkey ceremony's state in its two-factor cookie
    (`Identity.TwoFactorUserId`, as ASP.NET Core 10.0.3's `SignInManager` stores it), which lasts 5 minutes, the
    default that S00.04.05 sets explicitly with no sliding (D69). `PerformPasskeyAssertionAsync` throws
    `InvalidOperationException`, rather than failing, when that cookie is missing, has expired or holds another
    operation, such as a setup's ceremony or a password sign-in's code step ("No passkey assertion is underway…" and
    "Expected passkey operation…" in 10.0.3). Such a sign-in is a failed one on the credential's account, so a known
    credential writes `auth.signin_failed`, gets `401` and starts no session (the second and fourth criteria). The
    exception is caught only where Identity reads the ceremony: `Grow2NotesSignInManager` overrides
    `PerformPasskeyAssertionAsync`, the virtual method around that private read, and returns
    `PasskeyAssertionResult.Fail<ApplicationUser>(new PasskeyException(…))` when the base call throws
    `InvalidOperationException`, so `PasskeySignInAsync` returns `Failed` through its normal path. A catch around the
    endpoint's whole `PasskeySignInAsync` call is not used, as it would also turn an `InvalidOperationException` from
    saving the passkey or signing in into a `401`; those still become a `500`. A person meets a missing ceremony
    only after leaving the passkey prompt open past those 5 minutes, as the SPA fetches fresh options after 4 minutes
    and after any failure. The shared cookie also means that fetching passkey options replaces a password sign-in's
    pending code step, and the reverse. View A fetches options only while it shows (as it renders, after a failure
    and on a press with no fresh options), which is before any password is accepted, and view B has no passkey
    button, so neither interrupts the other. The exception is an options request still in flight when a password is
    accepted: its answer would replace the code step's cookie (task 9's Note to S00.04.05).
  - **Status and lockout on every runtime.** Of the two ways the Note above leaves, this story adds the check:
    `Grow2NotesSignInManager` overrides `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync`, where Identity's password and passkey sign-ins
    decide to sign someone in, to run `PreSignInCheck` first, so `CanSignInAsync` (Status Active) and the lockout
    apply to a passkey on 10.0.0 to 10.0.3 as Identity applies them itself from 10.0.4, at the cost of a second check
    of the same user on a current runtime. The reason is where the tests run: on 12 October 2026 a developer machine
    had ASP.NET Core 10.0.3 beside .NET 10.0.12, so `Environment.Version`, the .NET runtime's, would have said 10.0.12
    while Identity was 10.0.3. With the check, the tests give the same results there as in CI and Azure. 10.0.3's
    passkey path calls no `AccessFailedAsync`, so passkey failures are expected not to count towards the lockout;
    task 4's test records what it finds. CI runs a later runtime (10.0.12 on 12 October 2026), whose passkey sign-in
    checks the user before the override is reached, so there no passkey test reaches the override's check, and
    `Grow2NotesSignInManagerTests` calls it directly (the branch review's Note below).
  - **The options' answer** is Identity's options JSON as the response body, `application/json`, not wrapped as a
    JSON string, as SimpleWebAuthn's `startAuthentication({ optionsJSON })` takes it. `credentialJson` is the
    credential as `JSON.stringify` writes it (design.md §6.2's `{credentialJson}`) and is required, so a missing or
    empty one gets `422 validation.failed` rather than reaching Identity, which would throw.
  - **The passkey options** are design.md §8.4's: `ServerDomain` from `App:Origin`'s host, as S00.03.05's Notes
    suggest, so the setup links and the passkeys name one domain; user verification and a discoverable credential
    required; and `AuthenticatorTimeout` left at its 5 minutes (passkey-flows.md: never less). S00.04.02 needs the
    same options to create a passkey: whichever story is built first adds them, and the other reuses them. Built in
    task 1: where `App:Origin` is not set, as in CI's end-to-end run until task 7 sets it, `ServerDomain` stays unset
    and Identity takes the request's host, its own default. Since the branch review, where `App:Origin` is set, a
    credential must also come from a page at that origin itself, not in a cross-origin frame (`ValidateOrigin`,
    design.md §8.4 and A54).
  - **`200 Me`** is built from the signed-in user's row, the organisation's name and `MelbourneClock`'s today, as
    `/api/auth/me` gives them from the session, by one helper beside `MeEndpoint` that setup's and the password
    sign-in's `200 Me` use too.
  - **The start-up.** At start the SPA asks `GET /api/auth/me` once, as the `['me']` query of app-shell.md's *Refresh
    of `/api/auth/me`* (`staleTime: Infinity`, no refetch on focus). A `401` shows view A in the signed-out shell at
    the URL that was opened, a `200` the app, and a sign-in puts its `Me` in that query, so the app renders at the
    same URL with no navigation and no new history entry. This leaves out the `navigate(..., { replace: true })` of
    sign-in.md's *States* › Success, fresh load and *Interactions* step 5, as at the same URL it would change neither
    the URL nor the history; instead the shell moves focus to the app's `<h1>` when `Me` arrives after sign-in has
    shown, as that row asks. `/setup*` stays outside the start-up, as setup's views need no session (sign-in.md
    *Routes*): whichever of S00.04.02 and this story merges second keeps them outside it. S00.05.03 adds the
    start-up's loading line, its whole-page messages and `refreshMe()`; until then, a start-up that fails other than
    with `401` shows the header alone. S00.05.01 builds the signed-in header and the routes on the same query;
    whichever is built first adds it. S00.05.02 builds app-shell.md's route-change rule (one owner), which then meets
    the focus move above: a sign-in changes no location, so the rule does not fire for it, and S00.05.02 either takes
    the move into the shell's one focus owner or keeps it as the one exception (its Note from this story).
  - **The signed-out shell** is app-shell.md components 1 to 3 as they are when nobody is signed in: the skip link,
    the header with the wordmark as plain text, and `<main id="main-content" tabindex="-1">`. S00.04.02's setup views
    and S00.05.01's shell use the same, so whichever is built first adds it. View A's title, "Grow2Notes – Sign in",
    comes from app-shell.md component 3's `PageName` union through app-shell-nav.md's `usePageTitle` hook, and is
    the app's own again once the app renders. S00.05.01 builds the same union and hook: whichever is built first adds
    them, with every page name component 3 lists.
  - **The passkey helpers** follow passkey-flows.md's sketch, in `src/features/auth/` (`passkey.ts`): one options
    object, stamped with its time and fetched again after 4 minutes, once used or after any failure, and
    `cancelPasskey()`. S00.04.02's setup uses them too. Built in task 6, they differ from the sketch in three ways.
    The options are timed from their request, not their answer, as the server's 5 minutes start before the answer
    arrives. `prefetch()` joins a fetch already under way, and drops the options held as a new one starts, so no two
    option requests are ever in flight (passkey-flows.md *Anti-patterns*), StrictMode's second run of the effect
    included. Sign-in has no `isOurAbort`: its only cancel of its own is as the view unmounts, and a mounted check
    keeps the view silent after it; S00.04.02 adds `isOurAbort` for setup if it needs one. `@simplewebauthn/browser`
    (design.md §7.5) comes in with view A (task 5), whose notice reads its `browserSupportsWebAuthn()`, and the view
    then calls its `startAuthentication`: with fresh options, from the click handler with no `await` before it
    (sign-in.md *Interactions* step 2).
  - **A `429`** comes only once S06.01.05 adds the rate limiter (design.md §9.9). The view already reads one as
    sign-in.md does, as a failed sign-in, and its test stubs one. A `401` or `429` from any of the sign-in's requests,
    the antiforgery token's, the options' or the sign-in's, shows the A25 message, although sign-in.md *Sign-in
    failed* names only `/api/auth/passkey`: §9.9's limit covers every sign-in endpoint, and each such answer means the
    sign-in did not happen.
  - **The test authenticator.** The API tests register a passkey for a user they add, through
    `UserManager.AddOrUpdatePasskeyAsync` with an ES256 key the test makes, as setup stores one, and sign assertions
    over the options' challenge for the factory's `App:Origin` (`https://grow2notes.example`), sent as the request's
    `Origin` header, as a browser sends it; since the branch review, the app takes only a credential asked for at
    `App:Origin` itself (A54). With the user-verified flag off, signed with another key, over another challenge or asked
    for by a page at another origin, the assertion fails. S00.04.02's tests create a credential the same way: whichever
    story is built first adds the fixture, and the other extends it.
  - **The end-to-end test** uses Chromium's virtual authenticator through CDP, as passkey-flows.md's *Tests* say.
    With no Grow2Notes passkey on it, the prompt fails as a dismissal does; with a passkey that the test adds through
    CDP for `localhost` and no account holds, the sign-in fails with the A25 message. Its requests go through
    `api()`, so CI's end-to-end run must reach the app as HTTPS, or with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` from loopback, and
    set `App__Origin`, as S00.04.02's Note carried from S00.01.04 says: whichever story is built first does it. A
    passkey sign-in that succeeds in a browser needs a passkey that setup made, which is sign-in.md's Setup bullet
    "After setup, signing in a second time works…", linked in S00.04.02: whichever of the two stories merges second
    adds it to the end-to-end test.
  - **A form post to an endpoint that reads JSON.** Minimal APIs mark an endpoint that reads a JSON body as accepting
    JSON only, so routing passed `POST /api/auth/passkey` over for a request with another `Content-Type`, such as a form
    post, and the unknown-`/api` catch-all answered `404`, where S00.03.04's rule (design.md §9.8 item 3) asks for
    `415`. The `/api` group now removes that mark in a finally convention, as minimal APIs add it after the group's
    other conventions have run, so routing chooses the endpoint and its binding answers `415`. The endpoint matrix's
    form-body call to `POST /api/auth/passkey` proves it, and every later endpoint that reads JSON gets the same with no
    change of its own. S00.04.02's JSON endpoints need the same: whichever of the two stories merges second keeps one
    convention.
  - **Where things go:** the endpoints, their request type and the `Me` helper in `Features/Auth/` (design.md §7.3),
    the two overrides in `Grow2NotesSignInManager`, the `/api` group's convention in `Platform/ApiGroup.cs`, the test
    authenticator in `tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/`, and the sign-in view and the passkey helpers in the SPA's
    `src/features/auth/`.
- No copy needs approval: the hint, the help line, "Signing in…", the notice for a browser without passkeys and the
  "Not signed in: …" failures are sign-in.md's (P) strings of 9 October 2026, approved (D67), and "Sign in", "Sign in
  with a passkey" and the A25 message are design.md's.
- Signing in with a passkey in test needs an account set up there, which first happens in S00.04.02's after-deploy
  check (the operator's first `admin bootstrap` run, by hand). Doing it on a real phone and a laptop is F00.04's
  feature-level check, by the operator, so this story's after-deploy check signs nobody in, and its criteria are
  proven in CI. The options need no account, so that check still has a browser ask for them once in test (task 10, the
  branch review's Note below).
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.05 adds the "or", the email and password form and the code step to
  views A and B, with the error summary and the A25 message as built here, the `200 Me` helper, the audit rows' shape
  and the telemetry count; S00.04.08 puts the same passkey button in view C; S00.05.01 builds the signed-in shell on
  the start-up's `['me']` query, and S00.05.03 its loading line, whole-page messages and `refreshMe()`; S00.05.02 takes
  the focus move after a sign-in into its route-change rule, or keeps it as the one exception; S00.04.02 adds the second
  sign-in after setup if it merges after this story; S06.01.05's rate limiter adds the `429` the view already handles;
  and F05.02's table-driven audit test gives `auth.signin_succeeded` and `auth.signin_failed` a row each, triggered as
  these tests trigger them.
- Task 8's check, 12 October 2026, on the published Release build run as CI's end-to-end step runs it
  (`ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Production`, `App__Origin=http://localhost:5287`), on a migrated database of its own in a
  temporary LocalDB instance, removed afterwards, in Chromium 153 (Playwright 1.63's), driven with the keyboard only
  by a throwaway script outside the repository, the screenshot of each state looked at. As in CI, each request said
  it came over HTTPS (`X-Forwarded-Proto`). Chromium's virtual authenticator, added through the DevTools protocol as
  DevTools' WebAuthn panel adds it, answered the passkey prompts, and the script wrapped `navigator.credentials.get`
  to count them. The database held no account, and the console showed no Content Security Policy violation, only
  Chromium's lines for the expected `401`s, the requests made offline and `/favicon.ico`.
  - **Keyboard, no passkey.** On load nothing had focus and the title was "Grow2Notes – Sign in". Tab showed the skip
    link top-left, then went to "Sign in with a passkey", each with the 3 px near-black ring 2 px outside it
    (`:focus-visible`); the next Tab left the page, and Shift+Tab came back to the button. Enter, then Space, each
    started one prompt within 1 ms of the key, inside its user activation, with the options fetched as the view
    rendered. With no passkey on the authenticator, each failed as a dismissal does: no message, no summary, the
    title unchanged, no `POST /api/auth/passkey`, fresh options fetched, and focus back on the button with its ring.
    The authenticator still held no passkey at the end.
  - **A passkey that no account holds**, added for `localhost`: Enter on the button got `401`, and the summary showed
    the A25 message as its one unlinked item and took focus, with the ring 2 px outside its 4 px `--colour-error`
    border; the title became "Error: Grow2Notes – Sign in". Tab went from the summary to the button, and Enter again
    focused the summary again. The server logged "Passkey sign-in with an unknown credential" for each, and wrote no
    audit row.
  - **Offline.** With the options fetched and the browser then offline, Enter on the button gave "Not signed in: no
    connection. Try again." in the alert line above it, bold in the error colour, focus staying on the button with
    its ring. Enter again, with no options left to use, emptied the line, failed to fetch options and wrote the same
    sentence again. No summary showed, and the title gained no "Error: ".
  - **Throttled.** With DevTools' network conditions at 1.5 s of latency, the sign-in's `401` came 1.5 s after Enter.
    The button was `aria-disabled="true"` from 2 ms and showed "Signing in…" at 405 ms (not in the frame at 404 ms),
    which the page's status region also held. It kept its size, 229.9 × 48 px at 1280 px and 256 × 179 px at 320 px
    with text at 200% (the label there at 403 ms), and kept focus and its ring, and it was never `disabled`, as a
    `MutationObserver` and a sample on every frame showed. Enter and Space while busy started no prompt and sent
    nothing, and no animation ran.
  - **Reflow.** At 320 px with text at 200% (the browser's default text size at 32 px, set in its profile), at 1280 px,
    and at 1280 px with text at 200%, the page never scrolled sideways (`scrollWidth` equal to the width), with the
    summary, the alert or the notice showing; the notice with `window.PublicKeyCredential` removed, when the view
    asked for no options. At 320 px each wrapped in the 256 px column: the hint and the help line over 4 lines, the
    alert over 3, the notice over 10, the button's label over 3, and the summary's message over 14 inside its padding
    and border (184 px wide). At 1280 px the summary's message and the notice wrapped at the 640 px measure, over 2
    lines each, and the hint, the alert and the help line fit on one line each.
  - **Forced colours, emulated**, at 1280 px and at 320 px with text at 200%: the button drew its 2 px border in the
    forced text colour on the forced background, and its 3 px ring in `Highlight` when focused, as did the skip link
    and the summary, whose 4 px border also showed.
  - **Seen and left as it is.** At 320 px with text at 200% the skip link, shown on focus, wraps and reaches the
    window's right edge, so its ring shows on three sides there, not four. Focus stays visible (SC 2.4.7), and the
    skip link is app-shell-nav.md's, placed as its sketch places it, which S00.05.01 builds too; a gap on its right
    like the one on its left, such as a `max-inline-size`, is for the shell's own skip link, so that the lanes keep
    one.
  - **sign-in.md's accessibility checklist**, as far as view A's passkey part goes, this check shows: one `<h1>`, "Sign
    in", and the `<h2>` "There is a problem" in the summary; the skip link "Skip to main content" first, the `<header>`
    with the wordmark as plain text, one `<main id="main-content">` and no `<nav>`; `<html lang="en-AU">` and the
    titles, with "Error: " while the summary shows; the button's accessible name is its visible label; the keyboard
    alone does everything, in the order skip link, passkey button, with the 3 px `outline` on each stop, in forced

    status region and failures to the alert line, with focus kept on the button (whether a screen reader speaks them
    is the screen-reader pass); nothing is `aria-invalid`; the summary shows its errors by words and a 4 px border,
    not colour alone; the button is 48 px tall; reflow at 320 px and 200% text; and no motion. The screen-reader pass,

- 13 October 2026, branch review: seven findings were confirmed and are fixed here. Each new test was shown to fail
  on the change its finding describes.
  - The passkey options left `IdentityPasskeyOptions.ValidateOrigin` unset, so Identity took any credential whose origin
    matched the request's own `Origin` header, which a server that relays a credential sets as it likes. A browser lets
    a page on a subdomain of the passkey's domain, or at another port of it, ask for the passkey, so a compromised or
    taken-over subdomain of the production domain (A38) could have fetched options, had a person's passkey answer them
    on its page and relayed the answer to start that person's session; nobody can make a subdomain of the test address.
    Where `App:Origin` is set, the app now takes a credential only from a page at that origin itself, not in a
    cross-origin frame (design.md §8.4 and A54; A52 and A53 are S00.04.02's). In `PasskeySignInEndpointTests`, an
    assertion asked for at `https://www.grow2notes.example` or at `https://grow2notes.example:8443`, and sent with that
    page's `Origin`, is now a failed sign-in, where before it signed in. Identity checks a new passkey's origin the same
    way, so S00.04.02 has a Note on it, and design.md §7.2 now says that a passkey under the Vite dev server needs
    `App__Origin` set to the dev server's address.
  - Task 10's check after the deploy reached neither new endpoint, the first `POST`s behind App Service's front end, and
    view A drops a failed fetch of the options as it renders, so a `4xx` from them in Azure would have shown only at
    F00.04's check. Task 10 now has the operator open the test site once, signed out, and see the options answer `200`
    with the test host as their `rpId`, and a read-only query of `AppRequests` look for `400` and `415` on
    `/api/auth/*`.
  - Only the ceremony's end was tested, at 5 minutes and 1 second, so a two-factor cookie shorter than the 4 minutes for
    which the SPA uses its options would have left every test passing. A known passkey sent 4 minutes and 59 seconds
    after its options now signs in, and with the cookie at 90 seconds that test fails.
  - Nothing tested that a press while new options are on their way waits for them: with `prefetch()`'s
    `held = undefined` deleted every test passed, yet after a dismissal a quick second press would have signed the old
    challenge, whose ceremony the new request had ended, and got the A25 message. `SignIn.test.tsx` now holds the new
    options' answer open after a dismissal and after a refused sign-in, and checks that the second prompt opens with the
    new options. The first case fails without that line; the second fails only without both it and `options.spent()`, as
    each forgets the old options after a refused sign-in. `spent()` stays: the helpers mark options spent once their
    credential is sent, and setup uses them too.
  - CI runs ASP.NET Core 10.0.12, whose passkey sign-in checks the user before the override of `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync`
    is reached, so the passkey tests of a Deactivated, Invited or locked-out user guarded the override only on a machine
    with 10.0.0 to 10.0.3, such as the developer machine of 12 October 2026. The override stays, so that a passkey meets
    the status and the lockout on every 10.0 runtime, and `Grow2NotesSignInManagerTests` now calls it directly for each
    of the three, by reflection, as it is protected: with the override's check gone those tests fail on any runtime.
  - The Note *The start-up* gave app-shell.md's route-change rule (one owner) to S00.05.01; it is S00.05.02's, which now
    has a Note on the focus move after a sign-in.
  - The `/api` group's change to routing, by which a form post to an endpoint that reads JSON gets `415` rather than the
    catch-all's `404`, was not recorded; *A form post to an endpoint that reads JSON* above records it.

      onwards shows those two requests and no `400` or `415` on `/api/auth/*`; and Application Insights has no exception
      and no `5xx` from the deploy to the checks; record the run IDs and the query's result in Notes, and that the
      criteria are proven in CI; signing in with a passkey in test waits for an account set up there and is F00.04's
      feature-level check on a real phone and a laptop, by the operator (see Notes), so this check signs nobody in

---

## S00.04.05 Sign in with email, password and code
