As a worker who set up a password, I want to sign in with my email, password and a 6-digit code, so that I can sign
in on my phone or any laptop.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §4.1 *Signing in* and the "Wrong password or code…" row; §8.2 *Password*; §6.2's rows for
  `POST /api/auth/login` and `POST /api/auth/login/totp`; §8's *Two-factor code step* and *Lockout* rows, and §8.4's
  lockout options, `AddDefaultTokenProviders()` and two-factor cookie; §9.9's last sentence (the lockout counts
  password and code failures, but not a password attempt on an account with no password, A55).
- [design.md §14 M0](../../design.md#m0-skeleton-hosting-sign-in) *Done when*, "Successful and failed sign-ins appear
  in the audit log" (password sign-ins).
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Acceptance criteria › Sign in, every bullet not listed in S00.04.04 or
  S00.04.03: "Tab order on A…", "The passkey button is `type="button"`…", "Only "Sign in with a passkey" (A) and
  "Sign in" (B)…", "Empty email or password…", "A wrong password, a wrong code…", "After a failed password step…",
  "A correct password replaces the form…", "Pasting "123 456"…", "The code is never submitted automatically…", "A
  code `401` arriving 4 min 50 s…", "Code requests are never retried…" and "Offline, Continue…"; and the password
  sign-in's halves of four that S00.04.04 lists (its Note below): "after signing in, that route renders", "the
  password form still works", and "A second tap while busy…" and "The busy label appears only if…" for Continue and
  for the code step's "Sign in".
- sign-in.md *Components* row 1's "Error: " in front of the title after a failure, as the Note *"Error: " in the
  title* below reads it, and rows 2, 2a, 5 to 11 and 18, as far as view A's form and view B go; its *States* › Sign in
  rows Empty fields on Continue, Email has no "@"…, Busy, Sign-in failed, Code step expired, Code empty / wrong
  format, No connection, Server error and Success, fresh load, for the password and code steps; and *Interactions
  and focus* steps 3, 4 and 5, the Tab order of A and B, and where focus goes after a password is accepted and after
  the code step has expired.
- sign-in.md › Privacy and storage, its first three bullets, and › Layout and accessibility, its first two, each for
  view A's form and view B (the second's NVDA and VoiceOver pass is F00.04's feature check before M0 closes, as the
  Notes say).
- sign-in.md › Acceptance criteria › Setup, "After setup, signing in a second time works…", for the password path,
  which S00.04.03 left to this story as it merged first; its CI run then ticks S00.04.03's invariant criterion (task
  8).

Specific to this story:
- [x] `POST /api/auth/login` finds the account by its email and calls `PasswordSignInAsync` with
      `lockoutOnFailure: true` (`false` for an account with no password, A55), returning `{next: "totp"}` when Identity
      asks for the code; `POST /api/auth/login/totp` calls
      `TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync(code, isPersistent: false, rememberClient: false)` and answers `200 Me`; any
      other outcome of either, for a body whose fields are all there and not blank, is the same `401`, with no session
      (a missing or blank field is design.md §6.9's `422 validation.failed`).
- [x] Audit: `auth.signin_succeeded`, `auth.signin_failed` (known accounts), `auth.mfa_failed` and `auth.locked_out`;
      an unknown email adds only a count to telemetry, with no email.
- [x] Given 5 failed attempts on one account, passwords and codes together, when the right details are entered within
      15 minutes, then the same generic message shows and `auth.locked_out` was written (A25, design.md §9.9); wrong
      passwords for an account with no password, as one set up with a passkey, lock nothing (A55).
- [x] The code step lasts 5 minutes from the accepted password, and a wrong code does not extend it: after a wrong code
      at 3 minutes, the right code at 5 minutes and 1 second gets `401`, while one at 4 minutes and 59 seconds signs
      in (D69).
- [x] No path signs anyone in with a password alone: a user with a password whom Identity finds without two-factor
      gets the password step's `401` and no session (design.md §8.1 *Invariant*).
- [x] There is no "Forgot password", "Remember me" or "remember this device" (A22, design.md §8.2).

### Notes
- Identity's lockout options (design.md §8.4) are set here, because the generic message and `auth.locked_out` need
  them. This story's lockout test is the only one; E06 adds the rate limiter (design.md §9.9, S06.01.05) and does not
  repeat it.
- sign-in.md open question 6: set the two-factor cookie lifetime to 5 minutes explicitly, with a test, or return a
  distinct response for an expired code step. Settle before Ready.
- Owner answer, 9 October 2026: the code step lasts 5 minutes, set explicitly as the two-factor cookie's lifetime and
  proved by an integration test; after it the person starts sign-in again, and an expired step gets the same `401` as
  a wrong code, with no distinct response (D69, now in design.md §8 and §8.4). This settles open question 6 above.
  The two-factor cookie does not slide (`SlidingExpiration = false`, D69, design.md §8.4): Identity's cookies slide by
  default, and a wrong code sent after half the lifetime would otherwise renew it. The integration test sends a wrong
  code after more than half the 5 minutes (for example at 3 minutes), because an earlier one would pass even with
  sliding left on, then the right code after 5 minutes from the accepted password, which gets `401`. The sign-in
  password has no Show/Hide (D68), and the sign-in copy marked (P) in sign-in.md is approved as written (D67).
- From S00.04.02 (13 October 2026, its branch review): the same two-factor cookie keeps a passkey ceremony, from its
  options to its completion, and setup's view E reuses options for up to 4 minutes (`passkey.ts`), so
  `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests` pins the cookie's 5 minutes too, to the second, with Identity's default lifetime until
  this story sets it. Setting it explicitly keeps that test passing; if this story's test names the 5 minutes in a
  constant, whichever of the two merges second makes both tests use it, so the windows cannot drift apart.
- From S00.04.04 (13 October 2026), which built view A's passkey part, in `SignIn.tsx` (`src/features/auth/`), and
  the start-up that shows it:
  - **The bullets this story now takes.** S00.04.04 links five of sign-in.md's Sign in bullets as far as view A's
    passkey part goes, so the three that need the form are this story's, through its link to every bullet not listed
    there: "Tab order on A…", "The passkey button is `type="button"`…" and "Only "Sign in with a passkey" (A) and
    "Sign in" (B)…". Four of the five also have a half for the password sign-in, which this story takes too, although
    S00.04.04 lists them: "after signing in, that route renders", from "With no session, any URL shows view A…"; "the
    password form still works", from "With `window.PublicKeyCredential` removed…"; and "A second tap while busy…" and
    "The busy label appears only if…" for Continue and for the code step's "Sign in". As the passkey sign-in does, a
    password sign-in puts its `Me` in the `['me']` query with `setQueryData`, and the route's screen then renders at
    the same URL with its `<h1>` focused (`AppShell`); the busy guard and the 400 ms label are the shared `Button`'s
    `busy` and `busyLabel`, which `SignIn.test.tsx` tests for the passkey button, the label at 399 and 400 ms.
  - **The two-factor cookie.** A password sign-in's code step and a passkey ceremony share Identity's two-factor
    cookie (S00.04.04's Note *A ceremony that is missing or too old*), so the 5 minutes this story sets on it (D69)
    are the ceremony's too, and each answer that starts one replaces the other. View A asks for passkey options as it
    renders, after each passkey attempt that fails and on a press with no fresh options, and view B has no passkey
    button, so neither normally interrupts the other. But an options request still in flight when a password is
    accepted would, as its answer arrives, replace the code step's cookie, and the right code would then get `401`
    until the person starts again. This story guards against it, for example by sending the password only once no
    options request is in flight, or accepts it as rare, and says which.
  - **What its sign-ins reuse.** Their `200 Me` comes from `MeEndpoint.OfUserAsync`, the helper that the passkey
    sign-in uses. Their audit rows take the passkey sign-in's shape (`PasskeySignInEndpoint`): the person as both
    `ActorUserId` and the `User` entity, no `Details`, in the account's organisation, written after Identity's own
    save with no transaction, so that a failed write's `500` drops the session cookie. An unknown email is counted as
    `PasskeySignInEndpoint` counts an unknown credential, with "Passkey sign-in with an unknown credential": one
    `[LoggerMessage]` entry at Information from the app's namespace, with a fixed message, such as "Password sign-in
    with an unknown email", and no values, the email least of all. `PasskeySignInEndpointTests` shows how a test
    checks the rows and the entry.
  - **The screen-reader pass.** sign-in.md's checklist item "Tested with NVDA + Chrome and VoiceOver on iOS before M0
    closes" needs a person, so S00.04.04's check by hand (its task 8) left it for this story's form: a person does it
    before M0 closes, with the form, or in F06.02.
- From S00.04.03 (13 October 2026, its task 10): what this story takes from S00.04.03, whose Notes have the detail.
  - **On the server.** The password and key that the password path leaves: the key stored at the invite,
    `TwoFactorEnabled` set with the password, and no recovery codes, with `PasswordSignInAsync` then answering
    `RequiresTwoFactor` and issuing no session cookie (`SetupPasswordEndpointTests`). The authenticator token provider,
    which `AddDefaultTokenProviders()` registers in Identity's registration where design.md §8.4 has it, and with which
    `TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync` checks a code. Identity's password options as §8.4 sets them, `RequiredLength`
    from `Limits.PasswordMinLength` and no composition rules, beside which this story sets the lockout options. And the
    one code check, `OneTimeCode.TryRead` in `Features/Auth/OneTimeCode.cs`: NFKC, then whitespace, hyphens, dashes and
    minus signs removed, then exactly six ASCII digits, so that a badly formed code never reaches Identity and uses up
    no attempt.
  - **In the SPA**, in `src/features/auth/`: `PasswordInput`, with no toggle and `current-password` on views A and C
    (D68); `OneTimeCodeField`, to which view B adds a `labelIsHeading`, as `PasswordInput` has one through `FieldGroup`,
    to put its label inside the `h1`, which view G did not need; and `code.ts`, whose `codeFormatError` checks a code as
    the server does and whose `normaliseCode` gives the six digits to send. Their words are in `strings.oneTimeCode`
    (the field's label, which is also its empty field's message, and "The code must be 6 digits") and
    `strings.passwordToggle`, with view G's hint in its own group, so views B and C put theirs in sign-in's.
  - **In the tests.** `AuthenticatorCodes` (`tests/Grow2Notes.Tests/Fixtures/`) and `authenticatorCode`
    (`tests/e2e/authenticatorCode.ts`) compute an authenticator app's code from a key on the real clock, as Identity
    checks it (tests/README.md), and `setup.spec.ts`'s password path makes an account with a password and a key that it
    read from view G, ending signed in at `/`. S00.04.03's task 8 could not sign in a second time there, as
    `origin/main` had no `POST /api/auth/login`, so whichever of the two stories merges second extends that test, as
    S00.04.04 extended the passkey path's: with the cookies cleared, it signs in with the email, the password and a
    fresh code from the same key, and finds `GET /api/auth/me` answering `200` as the new manager. The passkey path's
    second sign-in passed in CI's run 38271751219 on PR #42, so this is the last of the two, and the run that shows it
    ticks S00.04.03's third criterion, with a Note there giving its ID.
  - If this story's branch has parts of its own for any of these, whichever of the two merges second keeps one, its
    users moved to it, as S00.04.03's task 10 looked for on its side after its last rebase.
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 after S00.04.04, as its plan allows
  (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and this story,
  the 5-point reference itself, it stays at 5: two steps on one screen, two endpoints over Identity, four audit events
  and a telemetry count, and the lockout and code-step tests on a fake clock, as releases.md describes it. S00.04.04,
  also 5, has built what it extends (view A, the start-up, the sign-in manager's override, the `200 Me` helper and the
  audit rows' shape), and the password and code fields and the code check may come from S00.04.03; against that, it
  adds the guard against a password alone and the wait for passkey options in flight (the Notes below). It is not
  clearly an 8 to split, nor as small as S00.04.01's 3. `depends_on` now names the stories whose code its tasks build
  on: S00.04.04 (the sign-in view, the start-up and `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync`), S00.04.02 (`AntiforgeryTokens` as it
  left it, whose `PostWithTokenAsync` the API tests post through, `useErrorTitle`, and `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests`,
  which task 3 changes), S00.04.01 (`Grow2NotesSignInManager`, `Me`, the session cookie and its test fixtures),
  S00.03.03 (the audit writer), S00.05.01 (the shell and the `['me']` query in which view A shows), S00.01.03
  (`Button`), S00.01.04 (`api()`, `ErrorSummary`, `FieldGroup` and `RequestAlert`) and S00.01.05 (the copy module).
  All are done but S00.04.02, S00.04.04, S00.05.01 and S00.01.05, earlier in this sprint (merged in PRs #43, #42, #41
  and #37, their checks after the deploy to come). It does not depend on
  S00.04.03, though it follows it in the backlog order, as S00.04.04 does not depend on S00.04.02: its API tests give
  a user a password, the authenticator key and two-factor through `UserManager`, as setup's password path leaves
  them, and its end-to-end tests reach the code step with no such account (the Note on them below); the parts the two
  share are built once, by whichever branch is first. `sources` adds D67, D68, D69, D71 and A35, which settle its
  wording, extras, code step, API types and test data; design.md §5.9, §6.1, §7.3, §7.5, §8, §8.1, §9.5, §9.6, §9.8
  and §9.9; app-shell.md for the shell it shows in; and form-validation.md, microcopy.md, passkey-flows.md and
  primary-actions.md for the form, the words, the shared cookie and the buttons. The linked criteria now list the Sign
  in bullets it takes, with the password halves of four of S00.04.04's, and link sign-in.md's components (component
  1's "Error: " in the title among them), states and steps for the form and the code step, its *Privacy and storage*
  and *Layout and accessibility* bullets for views A and B, §6.2's two rows, §8's code step and lockout rows with
  §8.4's settings, and §9.9's lockout sentence; the first criterion names the account found by its email and the one
  `401`, the third counts passwords and codes together, and two were added, for the code step's 5 minutes (D69) and
  for no password alone (§8.1 *Invariant*). No owner question is open for it (sprint-02.md, Definition of Ready
  check), and it adds no (P) string.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **The account behind an email.** `POST /api/auth/login` finds the account with `FindByEmailAsync`, which matches
    an address in any letter case, before signing in, so it sets the tenant from the loaded user (design.md §5.9 item
    1), audits against that account and calls `PasswordSignInAsync` with the user, not a user name. An email of no
    account is counted in telemetry as S00.04.04 counts an unknown credential: one `[LoggerMessage]` entry at
    Information from the app's namespace, "Password sign-in with an unknown email", with no values, the email least of
    all (§9.5), no audit row (§5.4: known accounts only) and the `401`, without asking Identity. Such an email is
    answered after that one lookup, while a known account also costs Identity's checks and an audit row, and one with
    a password a password hash, tens of milliseconds, and a save of its failed-attempt count, so the time an answer
    takes can show whether an account exists. Equal times would need the same hash and writes for every kind of
    account and for none, and a hash for an unknown email alone would only make an account with no password the quick
    answer instead, so the gap is accepted as design.md A56 and handed to F06.01's security review, with a Note in
    S06.01.01 (the review Note below).
  - **One audit event per attempt, from Identity's result,** in the passkey sign-in's shape (the S00.04.04 Note above):
    the person as `ActorUserId` and the `User` entity, no `Details`, in their organisation, after Identity's own save,
    with no transaction, so a failed write's `500` drops any cookie the response set. The password step writes none
    when Identity asks for the code, as no session has started; `auth.signin_succeeded` comes with the session, at the
    code step. A result that `IsLockedOut` writes `auth.locked_out`: the attempt that locks the account, in place of
    its `auth.signin_failed` or `auth.mfa_failed`, and each attempt refused while it is locked, right details included,
    so the third criterion's "`auth.locked_out` was written" holds either way. Otherwise the password step writes
    `auth.signin_failed` (a wrong password, or a user who may not sign in, Invited or Deactivated), and the code step
    `auth.mfa_failed` for a wrong code and `auth.signin_failed` for a user who may no longer sign in.
  - **The code step's account** is the one in Identity's two-factor cookie, read with
    `GetTwoFactorAuthenticationUserAsync` before anything else, and the tenant is set from it. With none, because no
    password step came first, its 5 minutes have passed or the cookie now holds a passkey ceremony, the request names
    no account, so it gets the `401` with nothing written; an expired code step leaves no audit row.
  - **A badly formed code** is refused before Identity, by the one code check that setup's code also passes (NFKC,
    then spaces, hyphens, dashes and minus signs removed, then exactly six ASCII digits), with the `401` and
    `auth.mfa_failed`, so it does not count towards the lockout (totp-setup.md *Implementation notes*). A code that is
    empty or only whitespace never reaches the endpoint: the body's `[Required]` refuses it with
    `422 validation.failed` and `errors.code`, and no event, as it refuses a blank email or password (*Blank fields*
    below); one of separators only, such as "- -", passes `[Required]` and gets the `401` and `auth.mfa_failed`. The
    SPA sends neither. Identity's authenticator provider reads a code with `int.TryParse`, which would take "12345" for
    the code 012345, so the check is the rule, not only a courtesy.
  - **No password alone (design.md §8.1 *Invariant*).** Identity signs a password user straight in, with no code,
    when it finds two-factor off for them: `TwoFactorEnabled` false, or no valid two-factor provider, as when the
    authenticator provider is not registered (`AddDefaultTokenProviders()`, which this story and S00.04.03 both
    needed, and `Program.cs` calls once). So `Grow2NotesSignInManager`'s override of `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync` also
    refuses (`Failed`) a sign-in that does not bypass two-factor when `IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` is false, and the
    password step answers its `401` with `auth.signin_failed`. Identity's passkey sign-in bypasses two-factor (ASP.NET
    Core 10.0.3's `PasskeySignInAsync` passes `bypassTwoFactor: true`, read on 12 October 2026), as a passkey is MFA on
    its own (D23), so it is unchanged. Setup and F00.06's reset keep a password and TOTP together; this keeps sign-in
    safe if a row ever breaks the rule.
  - **The lockout and the clocks.** design.md §8.4's options: 5 failures, then 15 minutes. Identity counts password
    and code failures together (§9.9) and, for a user with two-factor on, resets the count only once the code signs
    them in, so a right password between wrong codes does not clear it. It times the lockout on the system clock, not
    `TimeProvider`, as S00.04.04 found, and checks codes against the system clock too, while the two-factor cookie's 5
    minutes follow the app's `TimeProvider`. So the tests move the fake clock for the code step, compute codes from the
    real time, and read the lockout's end against the real time. The lockout applies to a user with `LockoutEnabled`,
    which `UserManager.CreateAsync` sets for every new user, as the invite core creates them. A wrong password counts
    only for an account that has a password (`lockoutOnFailure` is `HasPasswordAsync`'s answer, design.md A55): one
    set up with a passkey has none to guess, and five wrong passwords sent with its email, by anyone, would otherwise
    lock its passkey out (the review Note below).
  - **The 5 minutes** are one constant, which `Program.cs` sets on the two-factor cookie and every test of that cookie
    reads: this story's, `SetupPasskeyEndpointTests` and `PasskeySignInEndpointTests` (the Note from S00.04.02 above),
    so the windows cannot drift apart. The cookie's other settings stay Identity's, as §8.4 sets only these two.
  - **The shared two-factor cookie, guarded** (the S00.04.04 Note above): Continue sends the password only once no
    passkey options request is in flight, waiting for one that is, so that an options answer that arrives after the
    password is accepted never replaces the code step's cookie; the passkey helpers (`passkey.ts`) gain that wait, which
    starts no request. Nor does A ask for options while the password is out (task 5, from task 4's review): a passkey
    press with no fresh options, and the fetch after a failed prompt, wait for the password's answer and ask only if A
    still shows, so that no options request started during it can end after it; a test holds the password request open.
    View B asks for no options: as it replaces A, it cancels any passkey prompt still open (`cancelPasskey`), and
    nothing that A's passkey sign-in started says anything or asks for options once B shows, as `SignIn` asks for fresh
    ones after a failed passkey attempt while it is mounted, and B shows inside it; a test pins that no options request
    is sent while B shows. When view A shows again after the code step it asks for fresh ones, as on its first render,
    since the code step has ended any ceremony it held. Accepting the race as rare was not chosen: on a slow phone
    connection at the start of a shift, a password manager's quick Continue would meet it, and every right code would
    then fail until the step ran out.
  - **Blank fields.** The email is checked and sent trimmed. The password is never trimmed, but one of only whitespace
    counts as empty ("Enter your password", `value.trim() === ''`), as the server's `[Required]` refuses it with a
    `422` that no sign-in field shows; nobody has such a password, as Identity refuses one at setup.
  - **The form's messages** (task 4, 13 October 2026). sign-in.md's "an "@" with text both sides" is checked as
    form-validation.md checks an email elsewhere: text, one "@", text. A browser already takes spaces off the ends of
    a `type="email"` field's value, so the trim takes off what it leaves, such as a no-break space in a pasted
    address. A field that shows a message is checked again at each change, so its message says what is wrong with it
    now and goes once it passes, and a field with none gets none until the next press (form-validation.md rule 2).
    A press of the passkey button takes the A25 message out of the summary and leaves the form's messages, while
    Continue replaces them all, so a refused passkey's A25 message shows first, above the messages of an earlier
    Continue.
  - **The code step is view state, not a route.** View B replaces A's content in `SignIn`, in the shell's `<main>`, at
    the same address and with the same title, "Grow2Notes – Sign in" (sign-in.md *Routes*); the email, the time the
    password was accepted and the code are held in the view's memory only, never in storage, the URL or router state.
    Focus goes to the code field (sign-in.md *Where focus goes*), not to the `<h1>` that holds its label.
  - **"Error: " in the title** (sign-in.md component 1, and its checklist's SC 2.4.2 item): `ErrorSummary` puts it in
    front while the summary shows, on A's form for a client message or the A25 message, as for the passkey part, and
    on B for the A25 message; B's format message shows inline only (sign-in.md *Code empty / wrong format*), so
    `useErrorTitle` puts it in front while the code field holds that message, and takes it off as the message goes.
    The alert lines above Continue and above B's "Sign in" keep the plain title, as view A's passkey alert does on
    `main` (S00.04.04, pinned in `SignIn.test.tsx`), so that the sign-in views keep one rule.
  - **The code step** (task 5, 13 October 2026). View B is `CodeStep.tsx`, beside `SignIn.tsx`, which renders it in
    A's place, so its code, its message and its summary start empty each time a password is accepted; A's alert above
    the passkey button goes with A. Only a `401` or `429` from 4 min 50 s on ends the step (sign-in.md *Code step
    expired*): no connection or a `5xx` keeps B, with "Not signed in: …" above Sign in, as the step may still be
    running and the code can be sent again. The code's digits take `--font-size-h2`, 22 px on a phone and 24 px from
    40rem, as components take their font sizes from the tokens only (foundations.md) and none is 24 px at every width;
    sign-in.md and totp-setup.md, which asked for 24 px, now name the token (the review Note below).

- 13 October 2026, whole-branch review: eight findings were confirmed and are fixed here. Each new .NET test was
  shown, in a copy of the repository on a temporary LocalDB instance, to fail on the change its finding describes.
  - Anyone who knew the email of an account set up with a passkey could lock its passkey out. Five wrong passwords sent
    to `POST /api/auth/login`, which needs no sign-in, each added one to the account's failed-attempt count, and the
    fifth locked it for 15 minutes, its passkey included, again each time they were sent. Such an account has no
    password, so counting guesses at it protects nothing. The password step now asks Identity to count a failure only
    for an account that has a password (`lockoutOnFailure: HasPasswordAsync`); the attempt still gets the `401` and
    `auth.signin_failed`, and a locked, Invited or Deactivated account is still refused. design.md records it as A55,
    §6.2's and §9.9's lockout lines name it, the third criterion says it, and sign-in.md's open question 8 tells the
    owner, as it bears on whether a failed passkey shows the A25 sentence. A test in `PasswordSignInLimitsTests` sends
    five wrong passwords for an Active account with no password and a passkey: each is refused with
    `auth.signin_failed`, the count stays 0 with no lockout end, and the passkey then signs in. With
    `lockoutOnFailure: true` the account was locked.
  - Failed attempts on one account at once made all but one of them a `500` with no audit row, at both steps. Each loads
    the user before any saves its failed-attempt count, Identity's concurrency check refuses every save but the first,
    and Identity leaves the refused change tracked, so the audit writer's save sent it again and threw. On a failure,
    each endpoint now clears its context's change tracker before writing the event, as nothing else is left to save.
    Two tests in `PasswordSignInLimitsTests` hold each save of the user's row until every attempt has loaded it, then
    send four wrong passwords at once, and two wrong codes at once in one code step: each gets the `401` with its
    `auth.signin_failed` or `auth.mfa_failed`. Without the clearing, all but one got `500`. Identity then counts only
    one of the attempts (probed: 1 after four passwords, and after two codes), which the Notes added to S06.01.01 and
    S06.01.05 hand on: the limit of 10 a minute per account is what bounds guesses sent at once.
  - The Note on an unknown email's quicker answer named only the password hash, and handed the gap on in this story's
    Notes alone. A known account also costs an audit row, and one with a password a save of its count, while an
    account with no password costs no hash, so a hash run for an unknown email alone would only move the quick answer
    to those accounts. The gap is accepted as design.md A56, whose §4.1 row now says the timing can show what the
    words do not, and S06.01.01 has a Note on it (*The account behind an email* above). S00.04.08 needs no Note:
    sign-in.md's *Interactions* 3 and component 10 already give view C the wait for passkey options and a plain label.
  - S00.04.03 merged (e92ff93, PR #45) after this branch's last rebase, so the branch held second copies of the shared
    parts, its Note to S00.04.03 said the opposite of S00.04.03's own about the password path's second sign-in, which
    then had no owner, and no browser test signed in with a password. Two findings, fixed together: the branch is
    rebased onto e92ff93, keeps one of each shared part, drops that Note, and adds the second sign-in to
    `setup.spec.ts`, with its bullet in the linked criteria (task 8's Note above).
  - The code's digits are 22 px on a phone, where sign-in.md and totp-setup.md asked for 24 px, and task 7's record
    said 24 px. foundations.md keeps four type sizes, none of them 24 px at every width, so the specs now give the
    digits `--font-size-h2`, 22 px on a phone and 24 px from 40rem (sign-in.md component 10, its sketch and
    *Conflicts resolved* 29; totp-setup.md's *Code input* row and CSS sketch), and task 7's record says what it
    measured.
  - The first criterion and the Note on a badly formed code promised the `401` for any request, but `[Required]`
    refuses a blank field first, with `422 validation.failed` and no event. Both now say so; `TotpSignInRequestTests`
    and `PasswordSignInRequestTests` already show the `422`.
  - After the fixes, the checks in task 8's Note above passed, with the three new tests 8 times in 8.

## S00.04.06 Sign out

```yaml
id: S00.04.06
type: story
title: Sign out
status: done
points: 2
sprint: 2
depends_on: [S00.01.03, S00.01.04, S00.01.05, S00.03.03, S00.03.04, S00.04.01, S00.05.01]
sources: [D21, D22, D67, A28, A35, "design.md §4.0", "design.md §5.4", "design.md §5.9", "design.md §6.1", "design.md §6.2", "design.md §7.3", "design.md §7.4", "design.md §8.3", "design.md §8.5", "design.md §8.6", "design.md §9.6", "design.md §9.8", "ux/screens/app-shell.md", "ux/components/app-shell-nav.md", "ux/components/microcopy.md", "ux/components/primary-actions.md", "ux/components/settings-textarea.md", "ux/components/autosave-status.md"]
github:
azure:
```

As a worker on my own phone, I want to sign out from the Account menu, so that nobody else can use my session or see
what was on screen.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §8.5 *Sign-out*; §9.6, the sign-out half of its fourth bullet ("sign-out sends `Clear-Site-Data`"), as
  the rest of §9.6 is other stories' (see Notes).
- [app-shell.md](../../ux/screens/app-shell.md) › Acceptance criteria › Account and Sign out, every bullet but two
  parts: in the bullet about a waiting autosave, the `PUT …/draft` sent before the logout is S02.02.05's, which checks
  the bullet again when it adds autosave, and this story checks the reload of `/` and Back; the bullet about Guide
  prompts is S01.04.02's. The first bullet, which S00.05.01's tests show, is checked again with Sign out in the panel.
- app-shell.md component 2d's panel contents 2 and 3 (Sign out and its status line); the *States* rows Signing out,
  Sign-out failed: no connection, Sign-out failed: server and Signed out (own choice); and the *What happens on each
  action* rows Press Sign out and Sign out fails, but their leave-guard and autosave steps (see Notes).

Specific to this story:
- [x] `POST /api/auth/logout` ends this browser's session, while the person's sessions on other devices go on (see
      Notes): it answers `204` with `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "cookies", "storage"`, deletes the session cookie and
      writes `auth.signed_out`, with the person as actor and entity, in their organisation; with no session it answers
      `401`, sets no cookie and writes nothing.
- [x] After a `204` or a `401` from it, the SPA does a full reload of `/` with `location.replace`, so nothing of the
      signed-in screen stays in memory or in Back.

### Notes
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the
  reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and S00.04.05 (5 points), it stays at 2. Its six tasks add one
  endpoint over Identity's `SignOutAsync`, with one audit event and one header; Sign out, with its busy and failure
  states, in the Account panel that S00.05.01 built; an end-to-end test; a check by hand; the documents brought into
  line; and the after-deploy check. That is the shape of S01.02.06 Reword a goal, one control, one request and one
  audit event with their tests, and clearly less than S00.05.01 (3 points), which added the router, the shell and
  three pages. `depends_on` now names the stories whose code its tasks build on: S00.01.03 (`Button` and its busy
  label), S00.01.04 (`api()`, its antiforgery token, and its logout `401`, which raises no session-ended event),
  S00.01.05 (`src/copy` and `msg.notDone`), S00.03.03 (the audit writer), S00.03.04 (the `/api` group's antiforgery
  filter and the endpoint matrix), S00.04.01 (the sign-in manager, the session cookie and the test-only cookie sign-in)
  and S00.05.01 (the Account panel); all are done but S00.01.05 and S00.05.01, earlier in this sprint (merged in PRs #37
  and #41). `sources` now names D67, which approved its (P) words; A35, for its made-up test data; design.md §5.9 (the
  tenant from the session), §6.1 (`no-store`), §7.3 (where the files go), §7.4 and §8.6 (what rotates the security
  stamp, which signing out leaves alone), §8.3 (the `HttpOnly` cookie) and §9.8 (the antiforgery token); microcopy.md,
  for the words; primary-actions.md, for the busy label; and settings-textarea.md and autosave-status.md, for the
  steps that S01.04.02 and S02.02.05 add. The linked §9.6 was narrowed to what this story builds and the Account and
  Sign out group to its parts here, and component 2d's panel contents and the *States* and *What happens on each
  action* rows for Sign out were linked; the story's criterion was split in two, the first now saying what ending the
  session means, what a `401` gets and what the event holds, and the second how the SPA reloads. The rest of §9.6 is
  other stories': `no-store` is S00.01.01's, the memory-only query cache S00.01.04's, the empty storage and generic
  titles S00.05.01's, the setup token's removal S00.04.02's, the session limits S00.04.01's and the exported files
  E04's and E05's.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **This browser only.** The endpoint signs out through the sign-in manager (`SignInManager.SignOutAsync()`, which
    deletes the session cookie and Identity's other sign-in cookies), as S00.04.01's Notes planned, and leaves the
    security stamp alone. Rotating it would end the person's sessions on every device, which design.md gives to
    deactivation and a sign-in reset (§8.6) and to `admin signout-all` (§7.4, S00.03.06), not to Sign out; a manager
    signed in on a phone and a laptop signs out of each. The cookie is `HttpOnly`, so no script could have copied it
    (§8.3).
  - **The event before the sign-out.** `auth.signed_out` goes through `IAuditWriter` in the request's tenant, the
    session's `org_id` (§5.9 item 1), with the person's ID as actor and entity, `AuditEntityTypes.User`, and no
    details, with no transaction, as §5.9 lists none for a sign-in event. A failed write throws before the sign-out,
    so the exception handler's `500` has neither the cookie's deletion nor `Clear-Site-Data`: the person stays signed
    in, no sign-out goes unrecorded, and the panel says "Not signed out: something went wrong. Try again."
  - **`Clear-Site-Data`** goes on the `204` only, exactly as design.md §6.2 and §8.5 write it. A browser heeds it only
    from a secure origin, which the deployed app always is, and which CI's `http://localhost` counts as in Chromium.
  - **A `401` counts as done.** With no session, or one that has just ended, the fallback policy refuses the request
    before the endpoint runs, and before the antiforgery filter, so the `401` has no event and no `Clear-Site-Data`.
    With no session cookie, or one idle for more than 30 minutes, whose expired ticket the cookie handler ignores
    without deleting it, it has no `Set-Cookie` either (criterion 1, `SignedOutTests`, task 6). A session that the
    session check ends, at the 12-hour limit or on a changed security stamp, gets that check's `401`, which deletes
    the session cookie, as the check signs out (S00.04.01's `SessionLimitTests` and `NextRequestTests`). Either way
    no session is left to end, and the SPA's full reload drops what memory held. S00.01.04's `api()` raises no
    session-ended event for it, so the signed-out-in-place view (S00.04.08) never shows for it.
  - **The antiforgery token.** Signing out changes something, so it is a `POST` in the `/api` group and carries the
    token like any change (§9.8): `api()` fetches one first when it holds none, and a stale one's `400` fetches a fresh
    one and sends the request once more (S00.01.04). With `SameSite=Strict` too, no other site can sign a person out.
  - **The SPA's steps, for now.** app-shell.md's *Press Sign out* has six steps, 0 to 5. Steps 0 and 3, and the failed
    logout's `rearmUnloadGuard()`, need Guide prompts' leave guard (`leaveGuard.ts`, settings-textarea.md), and step 2
    needs autosave's `flushNow()` (autosave-status.md); neither exists before E01 and E02. So this story builds steps
    1, 4 and 5 and the failure path, with no stand-ins for the others, which would be dead code; S01.04.02 adds the
    leave guard's steps with `leaveGuard.ts`, and S02.02.05 the flush with its "Not signed out: your note is not saved
    yet…" line (task 5's Notes to each).
  - **The full reload** is `window.location.replace('/')`, in a module of one function, `src/routes/fullPageLoad.ts`,
    so that the Vitest tests can replace it with `vi.mock`, as jsdom does not navigate. `replace` keeps the signed-in
    screen out of Back, and the new page starts with empty memory, so it meets `/api/auth/me`'s `401` and shows the
    signed-out frame, or sign-in's view A once S00.04.04 has built it. S00.04.08's reload for a different person
    (app-shell.md) uses the same module.
  - **The panel.** After "Signed in as {name}", Sign out is the shared `Button` in its secondary style, full width on
    a phone and as wide as its label from 40rem (component 2d), with the busy label "Signing out…", which the `Button`
    shows after 400 ms, being `aria-disabled`, never `disabled`, while busy; then the `<p role="status">`, in the panel
    from its first render, so that it exists before any text is written to it. Sign out passes
    `announceBusy={false}`, as the panel's line says sign-out errors only (app-shell.md *Live regions*) and the busy
    label is not announced (*States*). Pressing Sign out empties the line first, so that a second failure is announced
    again, and the button stays busy after a `204` or a `401` until the page goes. The failure is microcopy.md §4's
    Action failed shape through `msg.notDone`, its cause from `classify()`: "no connection" for status `0` (a network
    failure, a timeout, or a token request that got no answer), and "something went wrong" for any other failure.
  - **The panel's layout** (task 2). Its three parts stack at every width, under the wordmark on a phone and under
    Account from 40rem, as app-shell-nav.md's laptop layout draws them; S00.05.01's panel, a row that wraps, would have
    put Sign out beside the name on a laptop. The line is `--font-size-small` in the text colour, not red, as
    empty-loading-error.md's status lines are, and takes no space while empty. A failure stays in it while the panel
    is closed and opened again, until the next press empties it.
  - **Where the files go.** `LogoutEndpoint` in `Features/Auth/` (design.md §7.3), mapped in the `/api` group beside
    `/api/auth/me`; Sign out in the panel in `src/routes/Header.tsx`; its words in `src/copy`, "Sign out" and
    "Signing out…" in `strings.ts` and the failure sentence through `msg`.
  - **HTTPS in CI's end-to-end run.** The SPA fetches the antiforgery token before it signs out, and over plain HTTP
    the antiforgery endpoint answers `500`, as its cookie is `Secure`. So CI's end-to-end run must say that each
    request came over HTTPS, as S00.04.02's Note from S00.01.04 asks before its first change. Of that Note's two ways,
    the simpler is the header, with no change to CI and no certificate: the browser sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`
    through Playwright's `extraHTTPHeaders`, which the app trusts from loopback, and Chromium keeps the `Secure`
    cookie from `http://localhost`. The setup and sign-in stories need it too, and it is one header, so nothing is
    built to share: the sign-out spec sets it with `test.use({ extraHTTPHeaders: { 'X-Forwarded-Proto': 'https' } })`,
    unless `main`'s `playwright.config.ts` already sets it for every test by then, when the spec sets nothing (task 3).
  - **The end-to-end tests.** Nobody can sign in in CI's end-to-end run until S00.04.02 or S00.04.04 has merged, so
    the sign-out tests answer `/api/auth/me` through S00.05.01's `serveMe` until the logout request is sent, then let
    it through to the published app. The logout itself reaches the app, which answers `401`, as no one is signed in:
    the path that counts as done, through the real token fetch, which needs the HTTPS above; the fallback policy
    answers before the antiforgery filter would run. The integration tests show the `204`, and a browser shows it
    with a real session once a real sign-in is on `main` (task 3).
- The made-up data: the integration tests sign out users that each test adds to organisation A, with `example.org`
  addresses (A35), so that each finds its own `auth.signed_out` by its actor; the endpoint matrix signs out the seeded
  users through the test-only sign-in, which has no cookie, adding one event a call and changing no seeded row
  (tests/README.md). The end-to-end tests use `tests/e2e/me.ts`'s made-up worker and manager.
- In test: signing out for real needs a session, and writes `auth.signed_out`, so the after-deploy check (task 6)
  changes nothing and meets only the `401` path, and the criteria are proven in CI. The first real sign-outs in test
  come with F00.04's feature-level check by hand (*Feature checks*), in which each person signs out with Sign out
  before signing in a second time.
- Its words are app-shell.md's: "Sign out" (V), and "Signing out…", "Not signed out: no connection. Try again." and
  "Not signed out: something went wrong. Try again." (P), approved as written on 9 October 2026 (D67).
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.08 reloads for a different person through `fullPageLoad.ts`, and
  leaves logout's `401` out of the session-ended event, as `api()` already does; S01.04.02 adds the leave guard's
  steps to Sign out, and S02.02.05 the autosave flush (task 5's Notes to all three); S00.04.07's warning closes the
  panel, Sign out and all, before it opens (app-shell.md); Release 2's Support page (F07.01) signs out through the same
  endpoint and button; and F00.04's feature-level check signs out for real in test.

      `await autosave.flushNow()` before the logout request, with the "Not signed out: your note is not saved yet.
      Keep this page open; retrying." line, and checks that bullet; then check that CI's run on the pull request passes
- [x] After the deploy to test, check with requests that change nothing: `/` serves this build's `index.html`, whose
      hashed script and stylesheet are the ones that CI's Build the SPA step wrote in the run the deploy took its
      artifact from, each answering `200`; `POST /api/auth/logout` with no cookie answers `401` problem+json with
      `no-store` and no `Set-Cookie` or `Clear-Site-Data`; open the test site in a desktop browser with its developer
      tools, with `/api/auth/me` answered in the browser by a made-up worker (Playwright's `page.route`) until Sign out
      is pressed: at 375 px and at laptop width, the panel shows "Signed in as …" and Sign out, and pressing it sends
      one `POST /api/auth/logout`, which test answers `401`, as no one is signed in, so nothing is written, and the page
      loads `/` again and shows the signed-out frame (or view A); the console reports no CSP violation and no script
      error but the expected `401`s; confirm the deploy's smoke test passed; and record the run IDs in Notes, and that
      the criteria are proven in CI

## S00.04.07 Warning before the idle sign-out

As a worker writing a long note, I want a warning before I am signed out for being idle, so that I can stay signed in
with one tap.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §4.0 *Sessions*, its warning; §8.5 *Warning*; §6.8, its last sentence, for the clock: every request that
  `api()` sends and the server accepts counts as activity, so autosave's will once E02 sends them (S02.02.05).
- [app-shell.md](../../ux/screens/app-shell.md) › Acceptance criteria › Session, these bullets, as far as the warning
  goes (see Notes): "With fake timers…", up to 30:00, when the warning closes and nothing is sent, the signed-out view
  with focus on its `<h1>` being S00.04.08's; "Stay signed in keeps the dialog open until `204`…", every part; and
  "Hidden for 31 minutes, then visible…", its `401` ending the clock, whose signed-out view is S00.04.08's. The bullets
  about a waiting autosave and an open Submit confirmation are checked when E02 adds them (S02.02.05, S02.03.01).
- app-shell.md › Acceptance criteria › Structure, privacy and naming, the first bullet's `dialog`, after `main` in the
  DOM order; S00.05.01 checks the rest of that group (see Notes). And › Visual and settings, for the dialog: its border
  in forced colours, its scrolling inside itself at 200% text, and axe on the session warning.
- app-shell.md component 6 (session timeout warning), every bullet; the *States* rows Session warning, Session
  countdown, Staying signed in and Stay signed in failed; the *What happens on each action* rows 28:00 idle on a visible
  page, but its autosave flush (S02.02.05) and its closing of an open `<dialog>` and of the note form's menu (see
  Notes), 29:00 / 29:20 / 29:40, Press Stay signed in, but its `401`'s signed-out view (S00.04.08) and focus after a
  closed confirmation (S02.03.01), Escape or Android back on the warning, Click the backdrop, Page hidden, and Page
  visible again, but its `401`'s view; the *Tab order* with the warning open; the dialog's two rows in *Live regions*;
  and the *Accessibility checklist* items for the dialog.
- [session-timeout.md](../../ux/components/session-timeout.md) *Behaviour* › The session clock, steps 1, 2 and 4 to 7,
  but step 7's signed-out view (S00.04.08); › The warning, steps 2 to 6, step 1's closing of other dialogs and menus
  coming with them (see Notes).

Specific to this story:
- [x] `POST /api/auth/ping` returns `204` and renews the idle timer, with `no-store` and no body, and writes nothing.
      With no session it gets the fallback policy's `401`, and past the 12-hour limit the session check's `401`, which
      deletes the session cookie.
- [x] No warning is shown for the 12-hour limit, which Stay signed in cannot extend (app-shell.md *Tensions* 2): the
      clock counts idle time only, so with a request accepted every 20 minutes for 12 hours no warning opens, and the
      `401` that then ends the session opens none.

### Notes
- From S00.05.01 (12 October 2026, its branch review): S00.05.01 left the `dialog` in the first Structure bullet's DOM
  order to this story, which brings the session warning, but nothing here named it, so no story checked it. It is
  linked above: at 320, 375 and 1280 px the shell's children are the skip link, `header`, the before-main bar slot,
  `main` and the `dialog`, in that order, with the dialog mounted once by the shell, outside anything that can be made
  `hidden` or `inert` (app-shell.md component 6; session-timeout.md *Per-screen notes*). S00.05.01's end-to-end test
  of that order (`tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts`) gains the `dialog` here.
- From S00.05.03 (13 October 2026, its task 7): the visible trigger. `refreshMe(queryClient)` in `src/api/me.ts` is
  the one caller of `GET /api/auth/me`: it shares a request already in flight and returns the request's promise, for
  the session clock to hold on, and its caller gives it the query client it has from `useQueryClient()`. Until this
  story, the page becoming visible calls it from a `visibilitychange` listener of the shell's own, in `useRefreshMe()`
  (`src/routes/useRefreshMe.ts`), beside the `location.key` and window `focus` triggers. This story moves that trigger
  into the clock, as app-shell.md *Refresh of `/api/auth/me`* and *Page visible again* have it: the shell gives the
  clock `() => refreshMe(queryClient)` as its `checkSession` (session-timeout.md *Implementation notes*), the clock's
  one `visibilitychange` listener calls `holdWhile(refreshMe(queryClient))`, and the hook's own listener goes, so that
  one listener sends the request and the clock holds on that very promise (*Conflicts resolved* 6). A `refreshMe()`
  that fails leaves the query's answer as it was, and the clock decides. `useRefreshMe.test.tsx`'s visibility tests
  (one request for visible, none for hidden, one for `focus` and `visibilitychange` fired together) and the request
  counts in `tests/e2e/start-up.spec.ts` keep passing through the clock.
- Sprint 02, 12 October 2026: refined to Ready and pulled into Sprint 02 (sprint-02.md, Plan). Sized again against the
  reference stories (releases.md), S01.02.06 (2 points) and S00.04.05 (5 points), and against S00.05.03 (3 points), it

  and S00.05.03, earlier in this sprint (merged in PRs #37, #43, #44, #41 and #47). `sources` now names D22 (nothing on
  the device) and D67, which approved its (P) words; A32 (48 px targets and the focus ring), A35, for its made-up test

- No open owner question changes it. app-shell.md question 5 asks the owner to confirm that the warning closes an open
  Submit or Discard confirmation as Go back before it opens, where confirm-dialog.md's *Behaviour* item 9 has the
  warning sit on top of it: that changes S02.03.01, which builds the Submit confirmation and checks note-form.md's item
  37, and the step that closes an open `<dialog>`, which this story leaves to the first story with a dialog (below), not
  what this story builds. Question 4 (a different person signing in after a timeout) is S00.04.08's; question 3 is
  S00.05.03's `location.key` trigger, which this story leaves as it is; questions 1 and 2 are answered (D73, D67).
  session-timeout.md records tensions only, ux/README.md's open questions concern other screens, and releases.md lists
  none for it.
- Chosen here, because design.md is silent:
  - **Pieces other branches touch.** The shell, `api()`, `src/copy` and sprint-02.md are shared with any branch built
    beside this one, so task 1 first fetches `origin` and rebases onto `main` if it has moved, and at the pull request a
    part that both branches changed is kept once, with the build and tests run again.
  - **Where the files go.** `PingEndpoint` in `Features/Auth/` (design.md §7.3), mapped in the `/api` group beside
    `/api/auth/logout`; the clock in `src/api/session.ts`, beside the session-ended event that it listens to, as
    S00.01.04's Notes planned and §7.3's SPA folders have no `src/session/`; the warning, `SessionTimeoutWarning` as
    session-timeout.md names it, in `src/routes/SessionTimeoutWarning.tsx`, with its stylesheet, beside the shell's
    other parts; and its words in `src/copy/strings.ts`. Each module that exports a component exports nothing else.
  - **The endpoint** answers `204` and does nothing else. The session check renews the session cookie on every request
    it lets in (design.md §8.4, A50), so the ping renews the idle timer as any request does; §5.4 lists no event for it,
    so nothing is written. It is a `POST` in the `/api` group, so it carries the antiforgery token (§9.8), with no
    policy of its own, so the fallback policy admits any signed-in Worker or Manager and gives a request with no session
    its `401` before the antiforgery filter; past the 12-hour limit, `SessionRules`'s `401` deletes the cookie, as on
    any request.
  - **Activity** (session-timeout.md *The session clock* step 1). `api()` gives the clock the send time of every request
    it sends whose answer comes and is not a `401`, the antiforgery token's and a change resent after a stale token's
    included. A `401` that ends the session raises the session-ended event, which ends the clock too; a `401` that ends
    none (a failed sign-in, an ended setup session or a logout, as `api()` already tells them apart) changes nothing,
    and so does a request with no answer. Any other answer counts, a `5xx` included, as the session check runs before
    the endpoint (step 6). Two answers renewed nothing and count too, as `api()` cannot tell them apart: one that App
    Service's front end gives without reaching the app, such as a `503` during a restart, and a `500` from a database
    failure inside the session check, whose stamp check reads the user's row on every request, less than 15 minutes
    after the request that last renewed the cookie (later, the cookie's own sliding renewal comes with the `500`).
    After either, the clock can be late, and the server's next `401` then decides, as the server's answer always wins
    (A57, which accepts it).
  - **A request in flight holds the clock** (app-shell.md *28:00 idle*: "If a request is in flight, wait for it";
    *States*: "nothing in flight"). `api()` holds it for each call, through `holdWhile`, which counts its holds, so that
    two at once, the visible trigger's and a request's, both settle before the clock decides. While held, the phase
    neither opens the warning, nor steps, nor ends, and then takes the step that the time gives. So the ping keeps the
    dialog open until its answer, and a countdown step due while it is out shows as it fails.
  - **The clock's life.** `startClock(checkSession)` returns the function that stops it, and `useRefreshMe()` starts it
    in an effect, for the shell's whole life, signed in or not, as the visible trigger has run until now (S00.05.03's
    Note above). Its state, the phase, the newest accepted send time and the count of holds, belongs to one start:
    `startClock` makes it afresh, active with the start's time as the newest send time (the sketch's module takes its
    load's), and the stop removes the listener and the timer and drops that state, so that a hold that settles after the
    stop changes nothing. While it is stopped, `getPhase` gives `active`, `requestAccepted` and `holdWhile` do nothing
    and no timer is set, so tests that never start it, such as `api.test.ts`'s, which count the timers left after
    `api()` calls, are untouched, and each test that starts it starts afresh. Its one `visibilitychange` listener calls
    `holdWhile(checkSession())` when the page becomes visible and stops the timer when it becomes hidden; a refresh that
    fails is caught, and the clock decides. Its one timer is set for the next mark only, worked out from `Date.now()` on
    each wake, so that a throttled or frozen page catches up, and no mark sends a request, 30:00 included.
  - **When the warning opens.** In a warning phase, while the shell shows a signed-in screen: `['me']` holds an answer
    and the route is not a setup page. So never over view A, the start-up message or setup (session-timeout.md *Where
    it's used*), whose own requests move the clock all the same; a sign-in is an accepted request, which starts it
    afresh. The dialog is always in the DOM, rendered by `AppShell` after `ShellFrame`, so after `<main>`, and opened
    with `showModal()` and closed with `close()` in an effect as the phase enters and leaves a warning.
  - **Before it opens.** The header closes the Account panel as the phase becomes a warning, as it does on a location
    change, and moves focus to Account if it was in the panel, as Escape does, so that the dialog gives focus back to
    Account, never to a hidden Sign out (app-shell.md *28:00 idle*; S00.04.06's Notes). No other dialog or menu exists
    yet, so the step that closes an open `<dialog>` with a plain `close()`, which every confirmation treats as Go back,
    comes with the first one the warning can meet, S00.06.04's Reset sign-in or S00.06.05's Deactivate confirmation,
    whichever is built first, and the note form's menu with E02; the autosave flush at 28:00 is S02.02.05's (its
    criterion). None has a stand-in here, which would be dead code.
  - **At 30:00, or on a `401`**, the clock has ended and the dialog closes. Nothing else shows until S00.04.08 builds
    the signed-out-in-place view for that phase: the screen stays as it was, as it does for a refresh's `401` until then
    (S00.05.03's Notes), and focus goes back where it was before the dialog. So this story checks the linked bullets up
    to the end of the session, and S00.04.08 the signed-out view after it.
  - **The dialog** is app-shell.md component 6's: `<h2 aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">` with the countdown
    sentence, naming the dialog; the `<p role="status">` from the first render; and Stay signed in, the shared `Button`,
    primary and full width, 48 px, `busy` while the ping is out, so `aria-disabled` and never `disabled`, with no busy
    label, as a dialog keeps its labels and says "Connecting…" in its status line from 1 s (primary-actions.md §4;
    `useDelayed`). Its press empties the line first, so that a failure that repeats is announced again. No connection or
    the 10 s timeout gives "No connection. Try again." (`classify()`'s `offline`); a `401` closes it as the clock ends,
    and any other answer as the clock starts afresh. Its look is component 6's, from foundations.md's tokens:
    `--colour-surface`, a 1 px `--colour-text` border (2 px `CanvasText` in forced colours), `--radius-m`,
    `--colour-backdrop`, `inline-size: min(100% - 2rem, 28rem)` and `max-block-size: calc(100dvh - 2rem)`, scrolling
    inside, the heading at `--font-size-h2`, the 3 px ring 2 px outside, and no animation (*Conflicts resolved* 17); no
    inline style, as the policy allows none (§9.7). `tokens.css` declares its tokens on `::backdrop` as well as `:root`,
    in the one rule, so that each value is written once: before Safari 17.4, Chrome 122 and Firefox 120 a backdrop
    inherits nothing from the page, so on an older iPhone `var(--colour-backdrop)` would not resolve and the page behind
    the warning would not be dimmed. Every build target (from Safari 16) knows `::backdrop`, so none drops the rule.
  - **Escape and Android back.** `cancel` is prevented and taken as Stay signed in. `close` is handled too, as Chromium
    may skip `cancel` with no user activation since the dialog opened: if the clock is still in a warning phase as it
    closes, it sends the ping, and if that fails, it opens the dialog again with the connection error.
  - **The words.** The four countdown sentences are fixed strings in `strings.ts`, with the typographic apostrophe that
    `src/copy` uses (microcopy.md §1): no plural helper exists, and four fixed sentences need none.
  - **The tests.** jsdom has no `showModal` or `close` (the probe below), so the Vitest tests give both to the dialog
    and check what the warning does with them; what the browser does natively (focus into the dialog, the page behind
    made inert, Tab kept on the button, Escape, the backdrop, focus given back) is checked end to end in Chromium. The
    end-to-end test signs in for real through setup, with a passkey on the virtual authenticator, so that the ping meets
    a live session, and runs the 28 minutes on Playwright's clock, while the server's own clock runs in real time.
- Refinement probe, 12 October 2026, in this worktree's `node_modules` and a scratch page: jsdom 29.1.1's
  `HTMLDialogElement` has no `showModal`, `show` or `close`, and computes a closed dialog `display: none` and an open
  one `block`. In Playwright 1.63's Chromium 153, a dialog that a timer opened with `showModal()` at 28 minutes on
  `page.clock` was closed after `runFor('27:59')` and open after one more second, with focus on its one button; Tab
  stayed on it, and a click on the backdrop did nothing; Escape fired `cancel` each time, kept open by
  `preventDefault()`, whether or not the page had been clicked before, so Chromium's `close` without `cancel` cannot be
  met there, and the Vitest tests dispatch it; and `close()` gave focus back to the element focused before. A `GET` of a
  `POST`-only `/api` path in test answers `404`, as an unknown path does, so only a `POST` would show the ping mapped
  there (read-only `GET`s of `/api/auth/logout` and `/api/auth/ping`).
- Its words are design.md's and app-shell.md's: "You'll be signed out in 2 minutes" and "Stay signed in" (V), and
  "You'll be signed out in 1 minute", "You'll be signed out in 40 seconds", "You'll be signed out in 20 seconds",
  "Connecting…" and "No connection. Try again." (P), approved as written on 9 October 2026 (D67).
- The made-up data: the integration tests sign in as A's seeded worker through the cookie sign-in, as the ping changes
  no row (tests/README.md); the Vitest tests use made-up workers and managers, and the end-to-end tests
  `tests/e2e/me.ts`'s, with, for the real ping, an organisation and first manager that the `invite` fixture bootstraps
  with a new `example.org` address (A35).
- In test: the ping changes nothing, but a real one needs a session, and the warning a signed-in page left alone for 28
  minutes, so the after-deploy check (task 7) answers `/api/auth/me`, the antiforgery token and the ping in the
  browser, and test receives only `GET`s; the criteria are proven in CI. The first real Stay signed in in test comes
  with F00.04's feature-level check, the operator's, on the laptop with NVDA, which task 6 adds to *Feature checks* as
  the Definition of Done's screen-reader spot check for this dialog.
- What later stories take from this one: S00.04.08 shows the signed-out-in-place view for the clock's ended phase, at
  30:00 on a visible page and on any `401` that raises the session-ended event, with the warning already closed, and
  checks the rest of the linked bullets, which task 6 links in its criteria; S00.06.04 or S00.06.05, whichever adds the
  first `<dialog>`, adds the warning's step that closes an open one; S02.02.05 adds the flush at 28:00 through the
  clock's hold; S02.03.01 closes the Submit confirmation before the warning, as app-shell.md question 5's answer has it,
  with focus then on Submit note; and E02's autosave counts as activity through `api()` with nothing more (design.md
  §6.8).
- 13 October 2026, task 1: run locally on a copy of the branch pointed at a temporary LocalDB instance, as Docker was
  not running, when the endpoint was added, and again on a new copy after the whole-branch review, with no .NET change
  between: `PingEndpointTests` (4), `SignedOutTests` (34), with Stay signed in's `401` problem with no session, and
  `EndpointMatrixTests` (7), whose ping row runs for each of its four callers, passed both times; after the review, so
  did the whole .NET suite but `HealthEndpointsWithDatabaseStoppedTests`, whose database the copy cannot stop (667
  tests). CI's run on the pull request, which is authoritative, is checked once the branch is pushed.
- 13 October 2026, task 2, the session clock as built. Chosen where design.md and session-timeout.md's sketch are
  silent or differ:
  - **The phases** keep the sketch's names, `active`, `warn-2m`, `warn-1m`, `warn-40s`, `warn-20s` and `expired`; the
    last is the ended phase that S00.04.08 shows the signed-out view for.
  - **The timer** is set for the next mark exactly, without the sketch's 50 ms more, so the warning opens at 28:00 to
    the millisecond; a wake that comes early works the phase out from `Date.now()` again and waits for what is left. A
    timer that fires while the clock is held does nothing, and the last hold to settle sets the next.
  - **A request counts once its answer's head has come**, before `api()` reads the body: the session cookie that the
    session check renewed comes in the head, so a body that then stalls, which `api()` fails as no connection, has
    still moved the server's 30 minutes.
  - **A `401` that ends the session** leaves the clock `expired` until a request is accepted again, as in the sketch.
    It is held like any other step, so `/api/auth/me`'s own `401` ends the clock as its call settles, at once.
  - **Stopping** tells the subscribers when the phase was not `active`, as `getPhase()` then gives `active`.
  - Also run locally, in Playwright 1.63's Chromium against the app published from this build, `/api/auth/me` answered
    in the browser: `tests/e2e/start-up.spec.ts`, its request counts included, and `tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts` passed
    with the clock's visible trigger in place of the hook's.
- 13 October 2026, task 3, the warning as built. Chosen where the design is silent:
  - **jsdom's stand-ins** for `showModal()` and `close()` are in `vitest.setup.ts`, for every test file, as other
    tests of the shell pass 28:00 on a signed-in screen (`useRefreshMe.test.tsx`'s 30 minutes left alone); `close()`
    fires `close` in a task of its own, as the browser does. The setup tests' Tab-stop helpers leave out a closed
    dialog, as the browser does.
  - **`isWarning(phase)`** is in `src/api/session.ts`, for the header and the warning.
  - **The header** closes the panel during its render as the phase becomes a warning, and moves focus to Account in a
    layout effect, which runs before the warning's effect: Chromium 153 leaves focus on a control just hidden until
    after then (probed), so it is still found in the panel.
  - **The heading** holds the 2-minute sentence while the dialog is closed, so that opening it writes nothing into its
    live region; the connection error is emptied as the dialog closes, so that the next warning opens with an empty
    line.
  - **Only no connection is said.** A `5xx` is an answer that starts the clock afresh, so the dialog closes on it with
    nothing said, as on a `204`.
  - **The look** takes confirm-dialog.md's padding (`--space-5` and `--space-4`, `--space-6` from 40rem) and
    `overscroll-behavior: contain`, and `max-inline-size: none`, as the browser's own cap on a modal dialog, the window
    less 6 px and 2em, would leave less than component 6's 16 px a side; forced colours draw 2 px of `CanvasText`.
  - **`tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts`'s frame order** gains the `dialog` here, task 4's first item, as the DOM change is this
    task's and the check fails without it. It and `tests/e2e/start-up.spec.ts` passed locally against the app published
    from this build.
  - **For task 4**, from a probe in Playwright 1.63's Chromium 153 on `page.clock` against this build: on a page that
    holds no antiforgery token, Stay signed in first fetches one, an answered request that starts the clock afresh, so
    the dialog closes even if the ping is then aborted; a test of the aborted ping aborts the token's request too, or
    holds a token first, as the Vitest tests do. Tab and Shift+Tab from the one button leave the page for the browser

  - **Four tests, each signed in for real** through setup with a passkey, by concern: the warning opening over the
    page, the clicks behind it, the countdown, Stay signed in, Escape and 30:00; the keys; no connection, with axe;
    and 320 px at 200% text, in a window 568 px tall, a small phone's, where the warning is taller than its

    `GET /api/auth/me` is the accepted request that the idle time counts from, so that each mark falls exactly where
    the test puts it: on a running clock, the real time that the steps take would move the marks. axe runs its own
    timers on the page's clock, so the no-connection test resumes it before axe, and checks the countdown a minute on.
  - **Tab and Shift+Tab** each take focus out of the page, to the browser, and the next brings it back to Stay signed
    in. As it comes back, the window's `focus` refreshes `/api/auth/me` (app-shell.md *Window regains focus*), whose
    `200` starts the clock afresh, so the warning closes, with focus back where it was, as on any request that the app
    accepts: the person is there, and the app's 30 minutes have started again. The design asks for nothing else, so
    it is left so, and the test holds that refresh until it has pressed the keys.
  - **A late `close` event.** Chromium fires a dialog's `close` a frame or more after it closes, and on the paused
    clock the warning opened again before the event of its last closing came, which the warning took as the browser
    closing it in a warning phase, and sent a ping. A `close` that finds the dialog open again is now left alone, with
    a Vitest test; in use, 28 minutes pass between a closing and the next opening.

      29:40, Stay signed in with the ping answered `204` closes it, and with the ping aborted it says "No connection.

      console reports no CSP violation and no script error but the requests failed on purpose, and the Application panel
      shows nothing in localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB or Cache Storage for the origin; confirm the deploy's
      smoke test passed; and record the run IDs in Notes, and that the criteria are proven in CI

---

## S00.04.08 Sign in again where I was

```yaml
id: S00.04.08
type: story
title: Sign in again where I was
status: new
points: 3
sprint:
depends_on: []
sources: [D21, D22, A24, "design.md §4.0", "design.md §8.5", "ux/screens/sign-in.md", "ux/screens/app-shell.md", "ux/components/session-timeout.md"]
github:
azure:
```

As a worker whose session ended while I was working, I want to sign in again on the same screen, so that I go back to
where I was without losing what is on screen.

### Acceptance criteria
Already specified elsewhere (linked, not copied):
- design.md §8.5 *No work is lost*.
- [sign-in.md](../../ux/screens/sign-in.md) › Acceptance criteria › Sign in again, in place, every bullet.
- [app-shell.md](../../ux/screens/app-shell.md) › Acceptance criteria › Session, these bullets: "Signing in as a
  different person after a timeout…" and "A `401` on Submit, Save changes, Discard or Mark reviewed is never replayed
  automatically…". The bullet "After timing out mid-note…" is design.md §14 M2's *Done when* line, owned by E02.
- app-shell.md › Acceptance criteria › Session, the parts of two bullets that S00.04.07 left to this story (see
  Notes): "With fake timers…", its signed-out view at 30:00 with focus on its `<h1>`; and "Hidden for 31 minutes, then
  visible…", its `401`'s signed-out view.
- app-shell.md › Acceptance criteria › Structure, privacy and naming, the second bullet, on the signed-out-in-place
  view; S00.05.01 checks the rest of that group (see Notes).

Specific to this story:
- [ ] Checked in M0 with the Invite user form half filled: after a `401` and signing in again as the same person, the
      typed values are still there and the URL never changed (app-shell.md component 7).

### Notes
- From S00.05.01 (12 October 2026, its branch review): S00.05.01 left the second Structure bullet to this story, which
  builds the signed-out-in-place view, but nothing here named it, so no story checked it. It is linked above: in that
  view the before-main bar slot, `#page-bar`, is `hidden` with the route, so that a back link or save indicator in it
  can be neither seen nor reached by Tab (app-shell.md components 3a and 7). S00.05.01 built the slot empty, so this
  story's test uses a screen on `main` that fills it, such as S00.05.02's user detail with its back link.
- From S00.04.06 (13 October 2026, its task 5): the reload for a different person (app-shell.md *What happens on each
  action*, *Sign in again, a different person*) calls `fullPageLoad()` from `src/routes/fullPageLoad.ts`, which
  S00.04.06 added for Sign out, rather than a `location.replace('/')` of its own, so that its Vitest tests replace it
  with `vi.mock`, as jsdom does not navigate; once S01.04.02 exists, `disarmUnloadGuard()` comes first. Sign out's
  logout `401` raises no session-ended event (S00.01.04's `api()`), so the signed-out-in-place view that this story
  builds never shows for it; keep it so.
- From S00.05.03 (13 October 2026, its task 7): a refresh's `401`. `refreshMe()` (`src/api/me.ts`) runs on each new
  location, on window `focus` and as the page becomes visible, signed in or not and on setup's pages too, and `api()`
  raises the session-ended event for the `401` of `/api/auth/me` (`onSessionEnded()`, `src/api/session.ts`). Until
  this story, a `401` that answers a refresh while `['me']` holds an answer changes nothing on screen, as any refresh
  that fails does, since the shell counts as signed in while the query holds an answer: `useRefreshMe.test.tsx`
  checks that the screen, the nav, the title and focus stay, with nothing written to a status region. This story shows
  the signed-out-in-place view for it (app-shell.md component 7, *Window regains focus* and *Page visible again*) and
  changes that test's `401` row to match. It shows nothing for a `401` that answers while sign-in's view A or a setup
  page shows, which need no session (that file's tests of view A and of setup's pages keep passing), nor for one that
  answers a request sent before a sign-in and lands after the sign-in's `Me`, which says nothing about the new session.
- From S00.05.03 (13 October 2026, its whole-branch review): a sign-in or a setup cancels a refresh of `/api/auth/me`
  still in flight before it puts its `Me` in `['me']` (`setMe()`, sign-in.md *Interactions* 5), so that refresh's
  answer never reaches the screen. Its request still carried the cookie from before, though, which the session check
  renews on every request (design.md §8.4, A50), so on a browser where another person's session was live, an answer
  that lands after the sign-in's can put that person's cookie back: every request then goes as the other person, and
  the next refresh brings them back on screen. It needs two people on one browser, one signed in while the other sets
  up there or signs in in another tab. This story, which handles a different person signing in, weighs it: a refresh
  whose `userId` differs from the one in `['me']` could reload the page (`fullPageLoad()`), as *Sign in again, a
  different person* does.
- From S00.04.07 (13 October 2026, its task 6): the session clock's ended phase. The clock in `src/api/session.ts`,
  which the shell starts for its whole life (`useRefreshMe()`), gives `expired` from `getPhase()` at 30:00 idle on a
  visible page, with no request sent, and on any `401` that raises the session-ended event, once that call has settled:
  `/api/auth/me`'s at once, with no warning first, the refresh as the page becomes visible after 31 minutes hidden
  included. It stays `expired` until a request is accepted again, as a sign-in in place is. The warning
  (`SessionTimeoutWarning`) closes as the phase leaves a warning, with focus back where it was, and until this story
  nothing else shows: the screen stays as it was (S00.04.07's Notes). This story shows the signed-out-in-place view for
  that phase (app-shell.md component 7), read as the warning reads it, with
  `useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, getPhase)`, and only while the shell shows a signed-in screen, as the warning
  opens: the start-up's own `401` ends the clock too, over sign-in's view A. It checks the parts of the two Session
  bullets linked above that S00.04.07 left to it: at 30:00 the view, with focus on its `<h1>` (S00.04.07 checked 27:59
  to 30:00, where the warning closes with nothing sent); and after 31 minutes hidden, the view for the refresh's `401`
  (S00.04.07 checked that no warning flashes, that the clock holds on that refresh, that its `401` ends the clock and
  that its `200` leaves it active). A `401` that answers a request sent before a sign-in and lands after it (the Note
  above) ends the clock as well, so this story weighs the clock with that case.

Written at sprint planning.
